# StorageInventory v1.1 (Persistent Inventory): pre-production specification

> **Status: planning authority only, final corrected revision.** This revision applies the required revisions RR-01
> to RR-10 of the G0 implementation-readiness re-review (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-rereview.md` at `35c841d`) to the
> repaired specification of `456b233`, which itself repaired `c0c031f` against the adversarial review at `d7f91bf`
> (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-review.md`). It is a candidate for the **focused G0 re-check of RR-01 to RR-09** that the
> re-review names (its §15). It is not implementation authority until that re-check accepts it (gate G0, §19). Nothing
> here changes v1.0 behaviour. v1.0 is described by the code on `main` and by the existing documents, which win over
> this file if they conflict. Items marked `OPEN` carry a safe default and the gate that must resolve them; no gate may
> start while it depends on an `OPEN` item that lacks a safe default.

The product transition this release makes:

| | Question it answers |
|---|---|
| **v1.0** | "What exists on this storage right now?" |
| **v1.1** | "What existed before, what exists now, what changed, and **what can we actually prove?**" |

The first implementation vertical slice is **scan → save snapshot → scan again → compare**.

**How to read requirement IDs**

| Prefix | Area | Prefix | Area |
|---|---|---|---|
| `INV-` | v1.0 invariants kept, and their narrower v1.1 companions | `SINK-` | Scanner observer contract |
| `REQ-` / `SREQ-` | Product requirements (MUST / SHOULD) | `CAT-` / `CAN-` | Catastrophic failures / cancellation |
| `LIB-` | Inventory Library | `SPOOL-` | Observation spool |
| `CONC-` | Library concurrency | `OBS-` | Observation window and the Library activity interlock (no overlap with Library mutation) |
| `ID-` | Storage/source identity | `IMP-` | Post-scan import |
| `FID-` / `NAME-` | File identity / name representation and comparison key | `DIFF-` | Comparison semantics |
| `SCH-` | Schema | `HIST-` / `SRCH-` | History, analytics, search |
| `LIFE-` | Snapshot lifecycle | `UI-` | Application |
| `PERF-` / `CAP-` | Performance budgets / capacity reassessment triggers | `SEC-` | Security and privacy |
| `BLD-` | Build and release | `TEST-` | Tests (§18) |
| `D-` | Decisions (§20) | `Q-` | Open questions (§21) |
| `REV-` / `R-` | Review findings and required repairs (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-review.md` at `d7f91bf`), dispositioned in §22.1 and §22.2 | `[E-n]` | Repair evidence (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-repair-evidence.md`) |
| `G0-` / `RR-` / `[G0 Rn]` | Re-review findings, required revisions and probes R1 to R6 of its §2.4 (`docs/v1.1-preproduction-rereview.md` at `35c841d`), dispositioned in §22.3 | `L-` | Documented limitations (L-ID1, L-ID2, L-DIFF1, L-DIFF2, L-OBS1) |
| `RX-` | Stated relaxations of v1.0 promises (§2.2) | `A-` | Security-audit rules (§16.4) |

**Facts versus proposals.** A statement that cites a repository path, a measurement, an inspected package or a
primary document is a **fact**. Anything labelled `DECIDED` is a design choice made by this document, subject to G0.
Claims that could not be independently verified are marked *(not verified)* and carry a gate or question that
verifies them. **Measured** and **projected** figures are always labelled as such (§15).

### 1.3 Dependency and build policy (v1.0)

| Aspect | v1.0 state | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Package references | **None.** `No_package_references_anywhere` fails the build if any `<PackageReference` appears | `tests/.../SecurityAuditTests.cs` |
| NuGet feeds | `<clear/>` of all feeds, nuget.org only, and **package source mapping** that allows exactly `Microsoft.NETCore.App.Runtime.win-x64` and `Microsoft.WindowsDesktop.App.Runtime.win-x64` | `nuget.config` |
| Runtime pack download | A conditional `PackageDownload` of the WindowsDesktop runtime pack for self-contained win-x64 only | `src/StorageInventory.App/StorageInventory.App.csproj` |
| Reproducibility | `Deterministic`, `PathMap` of the repository to `/_/`, `IncludeSourceRevisionInInformationalVersion=false`, `.gitattributes` forcing LF, and a Publish target that cleans first. The result is a byte-identical exe from any clean checkout | `Directory.Build.props`, `.gitattributes`, `build.ps1` |
| Toolchain | Repo-local .NET SDK 10.0.401, PowerShell 7.6.6 and ImportExcel 7.8.10 (tests only), each with a pinned hash | `tools/fetch-tools.ps1` |
| Single file | Self-contained, `PublishSingleFile`, `IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract`. The .NET host extracts WPF's native DLLs to `%TEMP%\.net\StorageInventory\<hash>\` | `build.ps1`, `docs/release.md` |

### 1.4 Native API surface (v1.0)

There are exactly **two** P/Invokes, both in `src/StorageInventory.Core/Paths/NativeMethods.cs`. They are enforced by
`The_only_native_calls_are_two_read_only_kernel32_functions`, which reflects over the Core and App assemblies:

- `kernel32!CreateFileW` with `desiredAccess = 0` and `FILE_FLAG_BACKUP_SEMANTICS`, to get a directory handle only;
- `kernel32!GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`, for the canonical location used by the alias checks.

### 1.5 Write, delete, rename and launch surface (v1.0)

| Capability | Only location | Guard |
|---|---|---|
| Create a directory | `ReportRun.EnsureOutputFolder` | Validated output folder only |
| Create a file | `ReportRun.CreateNew` (`FileMode.CreateNew`) | Only this run's six names, only directly in the output folder, and the folder is re-checked for being a reparse point |
| Delete a file | `ReportRun.DeleteOwnTemporaryFile` | Three proofs: created by this run, in the output folder, and exactly the temporary name |
| Rename a file | `ReportRun.PublishOwnWorkbook` (`File.Move(..., overwrite: false)`) | Same three proofs |
| Launch a process | `ReportOpener.Open` (ShellExecute) | Allow-list of this finished run's three CSVs, its workbook and its folder, and only on an explicit click |

The temporary Files file (`Files_<runId>.unsorted.tmp`) already lives in the report folder for the whole of a sorted
scan, is one of the run's own names (so the tripwire covers it) and is deleted by the three-proof rule on success; on
cancellation or failure it is listed as an incomplete artefact and never deleted silently. §11.7 reuses this pattern.

### 1.6 Public contracts (v1.0)

- **`IStorageInventoryScanner`** has the single method `ScanAsync(StorageScanOptions, IProgress<StorageScanProgress>?, CancellationToken)`. Its
  documented contract: the task "does not fault for scan, output or cancellation problems"; it throws only for
  programming errors such as a null argument.
- **`StorageScanOptions`** (`RootPath`, `OutputPath`, `SortFiles`, `ReparsePoints`, `ProgressInterval`) and
  `StorageScanProgress`.
- **`StorageScanResult`** has private constructors and the factories `ForFinishedScan`, `ForCancelled` and
  `ForFailed`. Its invariants:
  - `Finished` ⇔ `Complete | Incomplete`;
  - `Reports != null` ⇔ `Finished`;
  - `Complete` ⇔ `root.SubtreeComplete`.
- **Records** (`src/StorageInventory.Core/Records.cs`): `FileInventoryRecord` (raw values, UTC, bytes), `FolderInventoryRecord`
  (`Status`, `StatusReason`, `SubtreeComplete`), `ScanErrorRecord`, `ScanTotals`, `ReportSet`, `PhaseTimings` and `ScanFailure`.
- **Enums** (`ScanEnums.cs`): `ScanCompletionState`, `ScanErrorType` (the names match the PowerShell reference), `FolderScanStatus`,
  `ScanFailureKind`, `ReparsePointPolicy` (whose only value is `NeverFollow`) and `ScanPhase`.
- **Path policy:** `PathPolicy.Validate` returns `PathValidationResult` and `PathIssue`s with stable codes.
- **Reports:** `ReportCsvReader`, `ReportExplorer` (reads **CSV reports**), `WorkbookExporter` and `FileTypeCatalog`.
- **Internal seam:** `Scanning/IScanSink` has `OnFile(in FileInventoryRecord, int folderIndex)` and `OnError(ScanErrorRecord)`. It has **no
  lifecycle and no folder callback**; the folder table exists only inside `ScanEngine.Folders`. Its own comment says it
  "becomes public only when a second real consumer exists" (§11).
- **Failure handling (fact):** `ScanEngine.IsReadFailure` never classifies `OperationCanceledException` or
  `OutOfMemoryException` as a read failure. `InventoryScanner.Scan` catches **every** exception at its top level and
  returns `Cancelled` (cancellation with the token set) or `Failed` (`OutputInsideScannedTree`, `InternalConsistency`,
  `OutputError`, otherwise `Unexpected`). An out-of-memory condition therefore becomes `Failed (Unexpected)` today.
  §11.4 states how v1.1 keeps and bounds this.

### 1.7 Persistence and other behaviour (v1.0)

- **Nothing is persisted** apart from the reports the user asks for. There are no settings, registry entries, logs or caches, and the audit
  forbids `IsolatedStorage` and `Properties.Settings`.
- **Reproducibility:** as §1.3. The app's version is `1.0.0` everywhere.
- **The results screen depends on the CSV reports.** `ExplorationViewModel` → `ReportExplorer` reads `scan.Reports`. This
  matters for any "snapshot-only" scan (§3, D-43).
- **Report ordering ties:** the Folders report breaks size ties by folder discovery index and the sorted Files report
  by emission sequence (`ReportWriters.cs`, `SortEntry.Sequence`). Offline browsing parity depends on this (D-48).

### 1.8 Discrepancies found and how they are resolved

| # | Finding | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| X-1 | `docs/architecture.md`, "File identity", says NTFS file IDs "can change after defragmentation of some file systems or on copy". Microsoft's documentation says **NTFS** file IDs stay the same until the file is deleted. **FAT** file IDs derive from the directory entry's position, so they can change after third-party defragmentation or some renames. ReFS 64-bit IDs are not unique | Correct `docs/architecture.md` in gate C3 (§8 has the evidence) |
| X-2 | `IScanSink` has no lifecycle, and folders are never announced to sinks | Resolved by the C1 observer contract (§11.2): folders are emitted once, finalised, after aggregation |
| X-3 | The roadmap says "Persistent index: SQLite or equivalent, introduced only once snapshots need it". The request asks SQLite to *earn* the decision | §5 does the comparison. Consistent |
| X-4 | `MainWindow.xaml` is 388 lines, and the stages are `AppStage { Setup, Scanning, Results }`; scan state lives in `MainViewModel` | Decomposition is still needed before adding screens (C2), and scan state moves to an app-lifetime `ScanSession` (UI-12) |
| X-5 | The default Library location (`%LOCALAPPDATA%`) lies **inside `C:\`**, the most common scan source. The `c0c031f` design (D-09) wrote the Library during the enumeration of that source | **D-09 rejected** (REV-B01). Replaced by D-35: no Library write during observation, an out-of-source observation spool, and a post-scan import (§2.2, §6.4, §11) |
| X-6 | v1's top-level `catch (Exception)` turns out-of-memory into `Failed (Unexpected)` although `IsReadFailure` excludes it | Kept for the v1 entry point (behaviour preserved) and bounded by CAT-01 to CAT-05 (§11.4) |
| X-7 | `PathPolicy` blocks any source whose path passes through a reparse point (`SourceThroughLink`), which includes folder mount points; `c0c031f` §7.6 described folder-mounted sources as supported | §7.6 corrected: folder-mounted volumes are scanned through their own drive letter or not at all (D-42) |
| X-8 | README says StorageInventory writes "only inside the report folder you choose" | RX-01 (§2.2) adds the Library directory; README's "What StorageInventory writes" is updated when the Library ships (C5, SEC-18) |

## 2. v1.0 invariants and the stated relaxations

### 2.1 Invariants kept

All of these are `CONFIRMED` (enforced by v1 code and tests) and remain in force in v1.1.

| ID | Invariant | Evidence (v1.0) |
|---|---|---|
| INV-01 | The contents of scanned files are never opened or read. Only listing metadata is used | `ScanEngine` (`FileSystemEnumerable`), `SecurityAuditTests` |
| INV-02 | Scanned files and directories are never created, changed, moved, renamed or deleted, and their attributes and timestamps are never set | `SecurityAuditTests.File_creation_happens_only_in_ReportRun`, `Deletes_and_renames...` |
| INV-03 | Reparse points are never followed during traversal (`NeverFollow` is the only policy), and folders are re-checked just before they are entered | `ScanEngine.BecameReparsePoint`, `ScanEngineTests` |
| INV-04 | No administrator rights (`asInvoker`) and no elevation requests | `The_app_never_asks_for_elevation` |
| INV-05 | No telemetry, analytics, crash reporting, auto-update or automatic network activity. A network location is touched only if the user chooses it | `No_network_registry_dynamic_code_or_persistence_APIs` |
| INV-06 | No accounts, cloud services, registry writes, services, scheduled tasks or startup entries | same |
| INV-07 | Existing user files are never overwritten: creation is create-new only, and renames never replace | `ReportRun`, no-clobber race tests |
| INV-08 | Reports are never written inside the scanned tree (checked by validation before the run and by a tripwire during it) | `PathPolicy`, `OutputInsideScannedTreeException` |
| INV-09 | Incompleteness is explicit: per folder (`Status`, `SubtreeComplete`) and per scan (`ScanCompletionState`) | `FolderAggregator`, `StorageScanResult` factories |
| INV-10 | Cancelled or failed output is never presented as valid: there are no `Reports`, and created files are listed as incomplete and never deleted silently | `StorageScanResult.ForCancelled/ForFailed` |
| INV-11 | CSV reports are human and external-tool exports. They are **not** the internal persistence contract | `docs/architecture.md` "Reports vs snapshots" |
| INV-12 | The v1 report format (columns, formula guard, encoding, ordering) matches the PowerShell reference line for line | `ParityTests` |
| INV-13 | Scanning uses bounded memory: files are streamed, and memory grows with the folder count plus a 24-byte-per-file sort index | `CsvReportSink`, `docs/benchmarks/native.md` |
| INV-14 | Core has no UI, process, network or registry dependency | `Core_has_no_UI_process_or_network_dependencies` |
| INV-15 | The build is reproducible: a clean publish gives a byte-identical exe | `docs/release.md` |
| INV-16 | **UNKNOWN MUST NEVER COLLAPSE INTO MISSING.** Missing is emitted only where the newer observation proves that the item's namespace position was observable and absent; otherwise the result is Unknown. Added likewise needs proof from the older observation | `docs/integration-consumers.md`, `docs/roadmap.md`. v1.1 enforces it mechanically: §12.2 and §12.11 |

### 2.2 The stated relaxations, and their narrower replacement rules

v1.0 promises (README, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/native-security-review.md`) that StorageInventory writes
**only** inside the report folder the user chooses, which must lie outside the source; (INV-10) that files a cancelled
or failed run created are listed as incomplete and never deleted silently; that "the code references no packages" and
the only packages ever downloaded are Microsoft's two .NET runtime packs; and that the native surface is two read-only
`kernel32` calls. v1.1 relaxes exactly these four promises, each once:

| ID | Relaxation | Why it cannot be avoided |
|---|---|---|
| RX-01 | StorageInventory also writes inside its own **Library directory** (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\StorageInventory\Library\`, D-34). That directory can lie inside a scan source (for example `C:\`) | A Library outside every source would make the system drive unsaveable on single-volume PCs (§6.5, review §12.3 alternative A2) |
| RX-02 | INV-10's "never deleted silently" does not apply to the **observation spool** (§11.7), the one run file that is never presented as output: it is deleted automatically when its run ends, also on cancellation and failure | Keeping a machine-readable list of every file name in the report folder after a run that saved nothing has no value to the user and is a privacy cost; automatic deletion through the run's own handle is safer than v1's temporary-file pattern (SPOOL-09, SPOOL-14) |
| RX-03 | The code references exactly **four third-party packages** (`Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` 10.0.12 and SQLitePCLRaw 2.1.12 `core`, `provider.e_sqlite3`, `lib.e_sqlite3`) and ships **one third-party native library** (`e_sqlite3.dll`, SQLite 3.53.3), in one assembly (D-33, D-41). README's "the code references no packages" paragraph is replaced (BLD-18) | A persistent, crash-safe, queryable store needs an engine; §5 compares the alternatives. Replacement rules: A-09, A-11, A-20, BLD-02, BLD-03, BLD-15, SEC-10, SEC-12b, SEC-13 |
| RX-04 | The first-party native surface grows from two to **six read-only `kernel32` functions** (§7.8) | Volume identity needs four read-only queries on 0-access handles. Replacement rules: A-08, SEC-12a, ID-08 |

RX-01 and RX-02 are admitted only together with these rules, each narrower than the v1 rule it sits beside and each
machine-checked (RX-03 and RX-04 carry their replacement rules in the table above):

| ID | Replacement rule | Verified by |
|---|---|---|
| INV-17 | **Observation quiescence, both directions.** Within the StorageInventory process, an observation window (OBS-01: from before a scan's enumeration starts until that scan returns its result) and a Library mutation (OBS-08: anything that can create, write, truncate, rename or delete a file in the Library directory, including SQLite's own journal handling) **never overlap**: no window opens while a mutation is running, and no mutation starts while a window is open. The Library activity interlock enforces it (OBS-07 to OBS-13, D-51). It holds for every scan the process runs, whether or not "Save to History" is on and whether or not the Library is known to lie inside the source (containment can be unknown, LIB-06e, or hidden by an alias, SPOOL-01). **Scope:** one process. Another StorageInventory process (In use, CONC-02) opens no connection and never mutates the Library, but the owning process cannot see that other process's scans: limitation L-OBS1 | TEST-W1, TEST-W2, TEST-W5, TEST-A1 (A-16, A-25) |
| INV-18 | **Owned set only.** Library writes happen only inside the validated Library directory and only to the owned file set of LIB-02 | TEST-L4, TEST-A1 (A-01, A-04, A-05) |
| INV-19 | **Library-mutation tripwire.** A capture is never published (`NotEligible (LibraryChangedDuringScan)`) unless, between the opening and the closing of its observation window, (a) the interlock's mutation epoch is unchanged and the interlock stayed in this capture's Observing state, and (b) the database's commit generation (header change counter and page count, read from the file's contents) is unchanged (OBS-04). Directory-listing metadata is never used as evidence that the Library did not change | TEST-W1, TEST-W3, TEST-T1 |
| INV-20 | **App-data root blocked.** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\StorageInventory\` and everything inside it can be neither a scan source nor a report folder | TEST-L2 |
| INV-21 | **Capture data never lands in the source.** During observation, capture data is written only to the observation spool in the validated report folder (SPOOL-01), which v1's rules already keep outside the source and inside the tripwire | TEST-SP1, TEST-SP2 |
| INV-22 | **Spool deletion only by its own handle.** The spool is deleted only by closing the delete-on-close handle its own run created with `CreateNew` and held exclusively; never by name, never another run's file, never listed as output or as an incomplete artefact. Every report file stays under INV-10 unchanged | TEST-SP2, TEST-A1 (A-02) |

The zone model (§16.1) states the same thing per zone. The tree being observed is **read-only during observation,
always**; the Library, when it lies inside a source, is written only outside every observation window.

---

## 3. v1.1 product scope

### 3.1 MUST (v1.1.0 is not shippable without these)

| ID | Capability | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| REQ-01 | **Inventory Library**: one per user, at a fixed documented location, with its real path shown and one of the states of §6.8: Not created, Available, Missing, Leftover files, Unavailable, In use, Incompatible, Not a Library, Damaged | §6, D-34 |
| REQ-02 | **Save a finished scan as a snapshot** (Complete *or* Incomplete). On by default, with a per-scan opt-out. Saving starts only after the scan has finished observing (INV-17) and is atomic: a snapshot is published whole in one transaction, or is absent | §10, §11.8, D-35 |
| REQ-03 | **Stable source identity**: volume identity plus the root's exact location within the volume, independent of drive letters, with an explicit confidence and basis. Identity is re-verified at the end of every capture, and evidence weaker than §7 allows never attaches a snapshot to an existing source | §7, D-42 |
| REQ-04 | **Sources overview (multi-volume)**: every known source with when it was last saved, its latest totals and completeness, its capacity and free space at the last scan, and an online state that is checked **on demand** (Online, Offline, Not checked) | §7.7, §14, D-32 |
| REQ-05 | **History per source**: a list of snapshots in capture order with their totals and completeness, plus attempts shown distinctly and never as snapshots | §10.5, §13 |
| REQ-06 | **Compare two published snapshots of the same source.** Files are classified Added, Changed, Missing, Unknown or Unchanged; folders get an existence class (Added, Missing, Present, Unknown) and, separately, a content certainty. Counts and bytes per class. Unknown is determined mechanically and always carries its reason | §12, D-38, D-39 |
| REQ-07 | **Compare details**: largest additions, largest changes (by size delta), Missing, and Unknown with its reason, plus per-folder deltas marked **Exact** or **Not comparable** | §12.4, §12.8 |
| REQ-08 | **Browse a published snapshot offline** (the v1 results tabs, served from the Library): overview, largest folders, largest files, file types and errors, with the same rows in the same order as the CSV reports of the same run | §13.4, D-48 |
| REQ-09 | **Totals over time** as a data table: bytes, files and folders per snapshot, with incomplete snapshots marked "at least". (The chart is SREQ-11.) | §13.1 |
| REQ-10 | **Folder growth** between two snapshots: the top-N folders that grew or shrank, ranked only on Exact deltas; folders whose delta is Not comparable are counted and can be listed | §13.2, D-39 |
| REQ-11 | **Manual deletion** of one snapshot, with confirmation, and the Library's size shown | §6.9 |
| REQ-12 | **Attempt log**: cancelled, failed, not eligible, save-cancelled, snapshot-failed and interrupted captures are recorded as attempts. An attempt can never take part in a diff | §10.5 |
| REQ-13 | **Failure isolation**: a snapshot failure never invalidates valid CSV reports, and a scan that did not finish never publishes a snapshot | §10.4, §11.3 |
| REQ-14 | **v1 behaviour preserved**: reports, parity, safety and the preflight rules are unchanged whenever a snapshot is saved | §2, C1 |
| REQ-15 | **Safe Library failure modes**: a corrupt, incompatible, locked, missing or leftover Library is reported and never recreated, overwritten or deleted silently | §6.8 |
| REQ-16 | **Last seen** for Missing items: the older snapshot in the comparison is where each was last observed | §13.3 |

### 3.2 SHOULD

Every SHOULD kept in v1.1.0 has its own gate and acceptance criteria. The others are moved out of 1.1.0 by D-43 with
the reason given.

| ID | Capability | Release | Gate / reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| SREQ-01 | Filter search within one published snapshot: name or path substring, extension, size range and modified-date range (§13.5) | 1.1.0 SHOULD | C9 |
| SREQ-02 | Extension and category growth between two snapshots, from per-snapshot summaries (§13.2) | 1.1.0 SHOULD | C9 |
| SREQ-03 | "Not modified since" age cohorts by `ModifiedUtc`, with explicit caveats about what that timestamp proves (§13.6) | 1.1.0 SHOULD | C9 |
| SREQ-11 | A chart of totals over time, beside the REQ-09 table | 1.1.0 SHOULD | C8 |
| SREQ-04 | Snapshot-only scans (no CSV reports) | **1.2** | Moves the results screen onto the Library, and the spool still needs a working folder (§11.7) |
| SREQ-05 | Library backup (`VACUUM INTO` a create-new copy) and "Verify Library" | **1.1.x** | Adds SQLite-native file creation and doubles disk use; no migration in 1.1.0 needs a backup |
| SREQ-06 | Delete a whole source and all its snapshots | **1.1.x** | Useful, low risk, not on the vertical slice |
| SREQ-07 | Snapshot labels and notes | **1.1.x** | as SREQ-06 |
| SREQ-08 | Export a comparison to CSV | **1.2** | The first change-set interchange; belongs with the 1.2 integration contract |
| SREQ-09 | Compact the Library (`VACUUM`) after deletions | **1.1.x** | Needs a temporary-storage design (REV-M01) and free space equal to the Library |
| SREQ-10 | A "last seen anywhere in history" lookup for one path | **1.1.x** | Not on the vertical slice |

### 3.3 Evaluated items

| Candidate | Classification | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Persistent snapshots | MUST (REQ-02) | The central capability of the release |
| Storage/source history | MUST (REQ-05, REQ-09) | |
| Multi-volume overview | MUST (REQ-04) | |
| Stable storage identity | MUST (REQ-03) | Drive letters are observations, not identity |
| Snapshot comparison with the five file classes | MUST (REQ-06) | |
| Historical storage growth, folder growth, file-count growth | MUST: totals and folder deltas (REQ-09, REQ-10) | Cheap once snapshots exist |
| Largest additions and changes | MUST (REQ-07) | |
| Completeness history | MUST (REQ-05, REQ-09) | |
| Last-seen information | MUST in its minimal form (REQ-16); across all history 1.1.x (SREQ-10) | |
| Browsing while a removable volume is offline | MUST (REQ-08) | |
| Historical and offline search | SHOULD within one snapshot (SREQ-01); cross-history search DEFERRED | A filter model is fast enough (§15). A query language is not needed |
| Moved/renamed detection | **DEFERRED** | §8: it cannot be proven portably without per-file IDs, which v1.1 does not collect |
| NTFS file IDs | **DEFERRED** | §8: collecting them needs a different enumeration API (a scanner rewrite that affects parity) or a per-file open |
| Automatic retention | **DEFERRED** | Deleting user history automatically is a destructive policy that deserves its own review |
| Advanced query syntax | **DEFERRED** | A filter form is enough (§13.5) |
| Scheduled scans | **DEFERRED** | It would need a task scheduler or a background process, which INV-06 forbids |
| Incremental / USN journal scanning | **DEFERRED** | Full-scan costs don't justify it. The USN journal needs volume handles with more rights and is NTFS-only |
| Automatic online probing of every drive | **1.1.x** | New automatic I/O that spins up sleeping disks (REV-M12); 1.1.0 checks on demand |

---

## 4. Non-goals and deferred work

### 4.1 Permanent non-goals (unchanged from `docs/roadmap.md`)

- Reading, hashing or fingerprinting file contents; thumbnails, previews or media interpretation (EXIF and similar);
  perceptual hashing.
- Duplicate judgement, classification or organisation of content.
- Moving, renaming or deleting user files, and cleanup automation.
- Telemetry, analytics, crash reporting, accounts, cloud sync and auto-update.
- Requiring administrator rights.

### 4.2 Deferred beyond v1.1.0

| Item | Why deferred | Revisit when |
|---|---|---|
| A user-chosen or movable Library location; multiple Libraries | Needs persisted settings and a safe "move Library" operation (§6.2). A location outside every source would also remove RX-01's special case | 1.1.x |
| An in-memory observation spool | It would make capture memory O(files) and needs an explicit INV-13 amendment (review A5) | Only if C10 shows the file spool misses PERF-02 |
| Collecting per-directory case sensitivity during the scan | Needs an extra handle per directory (a scanner change with parity impact); v1.1's absence rule is sound without it (§12.2) | 1.2 (Q-16) |
| Move/rename detection and per-file IDs | §8 | A reviewed ID-capable enumeration design exists (1.2 candidate) |
| Automatic retention | Destructive policy | Real Library sizes are reported by users |
| Scheduled or background scans; USN journal; file watchers | INV-06; no measured need | Full scans are shown to be too slow for real users |
| Cross-snapshot search and a query language | No proven need | After SREQ-01 ships |
| Comparing snapshots of different sources, or merging histories across a reformatted volume | Identity semantics (§7.6) | User demand |
| Delta or deduplicated snapshot storage | Full snapshots are about 64 B per file observation (measured, §15.1) | A CAP trigger fires (§15.5) |
| Regenerating v1 CSV reports from a stored snapshot | Parity risk: `FullPath`, local-time rendering | 1.2 |
| A public integration contract (including a public scan-observer API), NDJSON export and a CLI | Roadmap 1.2 and later; the observer interface stays internal (D-46) | 1.2 |
| Library encryption | Needs a non-default SQLite build (SQLCipher); licensing and key handling | On request |
| "Show in Explorer" for historical items | It would launch paths read from the database (SEC-15) | Never for untrusted DB content; possibly for live verified paths in 1.2 |
| SREQ-04 to SREQ-10 | D-43 (§3.2) | 1.1.x or 1.2 as listed |

---

## 5. Persistence ADR

**ADR-001: the v1.1 persistent store.** Status: **DECIDED** (D-33, superseding D-01; engine configuration D-37,
superseding D-03), subject to G0. SQLite is retained. What changed from `c0c031f`: the native-library loading model
(REV-H01), the side-file model and journal mode (REV-H02, REV-M01, REV-M08, REV-B01), the verified package and engine
facts (REV-O01 to REV-O05), and the dependency boundary (REV-M13).

### 5.1 Context and constraints

- **v1.0's dependency posture:** zero package references, a two-package allow-list for runtime packs, byte-identical
  builds, and a machine-checked audit of every write and native API (§1.3 to §1.5).
- **The workload:**
  - 2,000,000 file observations and about 470,000 folders per snapshot on a real system drive;
  - tens of snapshots per source (§15 separates what was measured from what is projected);
  - **one writer**: StorageInventory's own process, which imports a finished scan in one transaction after the scan
    has stopped observing (D-35), deletes snapshots on request, and records attempts, never while any of its scans
    observes (D-51);
  - **readers in the same process**: the UI pages, which may read while a scan observes (§11.6, OBS-03);
  - queries: ordered comparison of two snapshots; aggregates per snapshot; top-N by size; substring search over
    names; per-folder totals over time.
- **Integrity:** a partial snapshot must never be visible (§10). A crash or power loss must never corrupt earlier
  snapshots. A separated or deleted journal must not silently expose partial data (D-47).
- **Observation:** the Library may lie inside the source being scanned, and must not change while that source is
  observed (INV-17).
- **Privacy:** the store holds file names, paths, sizes and timestamps (§16).

### 5.2 Candidates

| | Candidate | Packages (exact) | Native code shipped | Licences |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **SQLite via `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` plus SQLitePCLRaw, with the provider set explicitly** | `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` 10.0.12, `SQLitePCLRaw.core` 2.1.12, `SQLitePCLRaw.provider.e_sqlite3` 2.1.12, `SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3` 2.1.12. `SQLitePCLRaw.core`'s netstandard2.0 group declares `System.Memory` 4.5.3, which the .NET 10 SDK prunes by default for net10 targets (NuGet `PrunePackageReference` documentation, review [DOC]; C4 confirms through the lock file, BLD-03) | `e_sqlite3.dll` (win-x64), SQLite 3.53.3 [E-1] | MIT (Microsoft.Data.Sqlite); Apache-2.0 (SQLitePCLRaw); SQLite is public domain |
| B | SQLite via the `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite` metapackage (bundle) | A's packages plus `SQLitePCLRaw.bundle_e_sqlite3` 2.1.12 | same | same |
| C | SQLite via **Windows' `winsqlite3.dll`** (`SQLitePCLRaw.provider.winsqlite3`, or our own P/Invoke) | `SQLitePCLRaw.core` and `provider.winsqlite3`, or none | none (it's an OS component) | Apache-2.0 / OS |
| D | Our own P/Invoke to a bundled `e_sqlite3.dll` (no ADO.NET layer) | `SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3` only | `e_sqlite3.dll` | Apache-2.0 / public domain |
| E | `System.Data.SQLite` 2.0.4 | `System.Data.SQLite` (and its interop native) | `SQLite.Interop.dll` / native | The nuspec carries no licence metadata *(not verified further)* |
| F | **ESENT** (`esent.dll`, part of Windows) via `Microsoft.Database.ManagedEsent` 3.2.0 or our own P/Invoke | 1 or 0 | none (OS) | MIT (ManagedEsent) |
| G | **Custom immutable snapshot files** (sorted binary rows plus a manifest per snapshot) | none | none | ours |
| H | LiteDB 5.0.21 | `LiteDB` | none | MIT |

The package facts come from the nuget.org API and from the package files themselves, inspected in the review and again
in this repair [E-1]. SQLitePCLRaw 3.x removed `bundle_winsqlite3`; `SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3` continues in 3.x with
SQLite-numbered versions, alongside `SourceGear.sqlite3` (REV-O02). `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` 10.0.12 depends on
SQLitePCLRaw **2.1.12**. SQLitePCLRaw 2.1.13 exists; its win-x64 `e_sqlite3.dll` is byte-identical to 2.1.12's (review
[PKG]).

### 5.3 Assessment

| Criterion | A (chosen) | C winsqlite3 | F ESENT | G custom files |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| New packages | 4 | 0–2 | 0–1 | 0 |
| Native dependency shipped | `e_sqlite3.dll`, fixed bytes pinned by lock-file content hashes; SQLite 3.53.3; imports only `KERNEL32.dll`; fixed PE time stamp [E-1] | none. The engine version varies with the Windows build (3.51.1 on the probe machine) and no Microsoft documentation of it as a public app API was found | none (a documented Win32 API) | none |
| Offline, repo-local build | Yes, once the packages are restored under the existing allow-list mechanism | yes | yes | yes |
| `nuget.config` impact | 4 exact IDs added to source mapping, plus a lock file | 0–2 IDs | 0–1 | none |
| Reproducible build | Expected: fixed package bytes and a fixed native time stamp; C4 proves it with a fresh-clone hash (Q-08) | yes | yes | yes |
| Single-file publish | `e_sqlite3.dll` is extracted by the .NET host with the WPF natives (§5.6) | no change | no change | no change |
| Security-audit expansion | Moderate and fully enumerated (§16.4): one provider assembly with 164 P/Invokes into one engine, the SQL surface, one side file | Same SQL surface, plus an **unpinned** engine | A large P/Invoke surface of our own | Small, but all the integrity logic is ours |
| Deployment size | +1,978,880 bytes of native code plus the managed assemblies (about +2.3 MB before compression; C4 records the exe delta) | +0.3 MB | +0–0.5 MB | 0 |
| Schema migration | `user_version`, transactional DDL | same | manual | manual, per-file format versions |
| Transactions and crash safety | ACID; rollback journal with `synchronous=FULL` is durable at commit ([E-5], pager.c 2127–2136) | same | yes (log files) | Ours to design |
| Corruption detection and recovery | `quick_check` / `integrity_check`; hot-journal rollback at open | same | `esentutl` concepts, in our own code | Ours (checksums) |
| Scale at 2M rows per snapshot | **Measured (§15.1)** on a proxy engine: about 190k rows/s written, 64 B per row, 3.3 s ordered comparison, 0.56 s search | same engine | not measured | the merge diff would be similar; search would be a full scan every time |
| Query capability | SQL: joins, aggregates, top-N, LIKE | same | Index cursors only | Hand-written |
| Maintenance burden | Low: a mature engine, and Microsoft maintains the ADO.NET provider | medium: behaviour differs between OS builds | high | high |

### 5.4 Decision (D-33)

**Choose A**: SQLite through `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` 10.0.12, with SQLitePCLRaw 2.1.12 (`core`,
`provider.e_sqlite3`, `lib.e_sqlite3`) and the provider set **explicitly** at start-up
(`SQLitePCL.raw.SetProvider(new SQLite3Provider_e_sqlite3())`) before any connection is constructed, rather than
through the bundle's batteries initialiser.

Reasons:

1. **The engine is pinned and the same everywhere.** CI, contributors and users run SQLite 3.53.3 (source id
   `2026-06-26 20:14:12 d4c0e51e…`), so behaviour and test results carry over. Option C cannot promise this.
2. **SQL makes the MUST scope simple and fast.** The measured comparison, top-N, search and aggregate timings (§15)
   meet the budgets without custom indexes or custom storage code. Options F and G would put a storage engine inside
   the product's safety perimeter.
3. **Durability and corruption behaviour are documented** by SQLite and confirmed in its source ([E-5]), so the
   lifecycle in §10 rests on published guarantees.
4. **The dependency stays contained** in the SQLite adapter assembly `StorageInventory.Library`. The comparison,
   history and identity semantics live in package-free code (D-41), and App receives the packages' runtime assets but
   not their compile-time assets (`PrivateAssets="compile"`).

Rejected:

- **B:** it adds one package (`bundle_e_sqlite3`) for an initialiser A does not need. (Microsoft.Data.Sqlite's own
  static constructor already probes for the batteries type by reflection whether or not B is used [E-1], so
  "reflection" is not a differentiator; the extra package is.)
- **C:** the engine is unpinned and differs by OS build; no documented app-level support was found; SQLitePCLRaw
  dropped the bundle. It stays the fallback if a bundled native DLL is ever unacceptable.
- **D:** more of our own unsafe interop code, to save one managed layer. A is safer to review.
- **E:** unclear licence metadata, and a non-standard native layout.
- **F:** the ISAM API has no query language, so we would write and own large amounts of unsafe interop.
- **G:** we would own format design, integrity checking, indexing and search. It is attractive for a future export
  format (1.2), not for the store.
- **H:** unproven at this scale and a weaker query model; a third-party managed dependency for no gain over A.

### 5.5 Package, engine and licence facts (verified [E-1])

| Fact | Value | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| Dependency graph | `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` 10.0.12 → `SQLitePCLRaw.core` 2.1.12 → (`System.Memory` 4.5.3, netstandard2.0 only; pruned on net10). `SQLitePCLRaw.provider.e_sqlite3` 2.1.12 → `SQLitePCLRaw.core` 2.1.12. `SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3` 2.1.12 has no dependencies | The lock file must contain exactly these four packages; `System.Memory` appearing is a **stop condition** for C4, never a silent addition (BLD-03) |
| Provider asset | A net10.0-windows project selects `lib/net6.0-windows7.0`. It imports the module `e_sqlite3` (164 distinct `sqlite3_*` entry points) and registers no DLL import resolver | The third-party native surface is exactly one provider assembly and one module (SEC-12b) |
| Native binary | `runtimes/win-x64/native/e_sqlite3.dll`, 1,978,880 bytes, SQLite **3.53.3**, source id `2026-06-26 20:14:12 d4c0e51e4aeb96955b99185ab9cde75c339e2c29c3f3f12428d364a10d782c62` | C4 asserts `sqlite_version() = '3.53.3'` and `sqlite_source_id()` exactly at run time (BLD-15), not a lower bound |
| C runtime | The DLL's only PE import is `KERNEL32.dll` | **No Visual C++ runtime is needed** (Q-09 resolved). The clean-VM smoke run stays a C12 release check |
| Reproducibility | PE time stamp fixed at 2010-01-01; package bytes pinned by lock-file hashes | Q-08 is low risk; C4 proves it |
| Compile options | `THREADSAFE=1`, `TEMP_STORE=1` (file by default, `PRAGMA temp_store` may override), `DEFAULT_SYNCHRONOUS=2`, `DEFAULT_FOREIGN_KEYS`, FTS3/4/5, RTREE, SESSION, math functions, `MAX_ATTACHED=10`; `sqlite3_load_extension` is exported | Extension loading is compiled in and must be kept off by rule (SEC-17, A-07); `PRAGMA temp_store=MEMORY` is honoured (§5.8) |
| MSBuild imports | `SQLitePCLRaw.lib.e_sqlite3` carries `buildTransitive` targets for net461 to net9.0; the net8.0/net9.0 targets act only for `RuntimeIdentifier == browser-wasm` | Package-supplied build logic, inert for win-x64; recorded here and pinned by the lock file (REV-O03) |
| Reflection in the provider | `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite`'s static constructor looks up `SQLitePCL.Batteries_V2, SQLitePCLRaw.batteries_v2` by name and probes the WinRT `ApplicationData` folders, which can call `sqlite3_win32_set_directory` in packaged apps | Harmless for an unpackaged desktop app; `SetProvider` must run before the first `SqliteConnection` (A-10) |
| Default open mode | `SqliteConnectionStringBuilder.Mode` defaults to `ReadWriteCreate` (review [DOC]) | `Mode` must always be set explicitly (A-06) |
| Licences | Package licence expressions only; **no LICENSE or NOTICE file** in any of the four packages | `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` carries the MIT text for Microsoft.Data.Sqlite, the full Apache-2.0 text for SQLitePCLRaw (no NOTICE text exists to carry), and SQLite's public-domain dedication (BLD-05) |
| Pin rule | The versions are the lowest SQLitePCLRaw version that `Microsoft.Data.Sqlite.Core` 10.0.12 requires | Any upgrade (2.1.13, 3.x, `SourceGear.sqlite3`, a newer Microsoft.Data.Sqlite) is a separate reviewed change (BLD-15) |

### 5.6 Native library loading model (replaces the `c0c031f` SEC-13 text)

**Facts.** In a self-contained single-file publish with `IncludeNativeLibrariesForSelfExtract=true`, the .NET host
extracts bundled native files to a directory under `%TEMP%\.net` (`docs/release.md`: `%TEMP%\.net\StorageInventory\<hash>\`),
and the runtime probes that directory for `DllImport` modules. `AppContext.BaseDirectory` is the folder **next to the
executable** (dotnet/docs single-file overview, review [DOC]); for a downloaded exe that is usually `Downloads`. The
`c0c031f` rule "load by full path from `AppContext.BaseDirectory`" pointed at the wrong directory and, if it fell back
to default probing when the file was absent there, would have preferred a DLL planted beside the exe (REV-H01).

**Decision (part of D-33).**

1. **No custom resolver in 1.1.0.** StorageInventory registers no `DllImportResolver`, calls no `NativeLibrary.*`
   API and never uses `AppContext.BaseDirectory` for native loading (A-10). The runtime's own single-file probing loads
   `e_sqlite3.dll` from the extraction directory, exactly as it does for WPF's natives in v1.0.
2. **What is verified, and where** (BLD-07, BLD-10, BLD-14):
   - at release (smoke test, TEST-R2): the loaded `e_sqlite3.dll` module path is inside the extraction directory under
     `%TEMP%\.net\StorageInventory\`; its SHA-256 equals the package's `runtimes/win-x64/native/e_sqlite3.dll`;
   - negative case (TEST-R2): a dummy `e_sqlite3.dll` placed beside the copied exe is **not** loaded and the app still
     opens its Library;
   - at run time (every Library open): `sqlite_version()` and `sqlite_source_id()` equal the pinned values, otherwise
     the Library is Unavailable ("unexpected SQLite engine") and nothing is written;
   - in CI (TEST-R3): the set of native files in the publish output equals v1's WPF natives plus `e_sqlite3.dll`.
3. **What is not claimed.** The .NET host does not verify the contents of an already-extracted file before reusing it
   *(not verified; no documentation found that it does)*, and StorageInventory adds no integrity check of its own. A
   same-user attacker who can write `%TEMP%\.net\…` could replace the DLL, as they could replace v1's WPF natives or
   the exe itself. That is residual risk SEC-24, documented, not mitigated.
4. **Q-11** (C4) confirms on Windows, with the exact experiment in §21, that the extraction directory is probed first
   and that the executable's folder is not probed while the extracted file exists. If it is, C4 stops and this section
   is amended (a resolver that takes the directory from the runtime's native search directories is the prepared
   alternative, reviewed before use).

### 5.7 Consequences (implemented only in gate C4)

| Area | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Packages | **`<PackageReference>` appears for the first time**, only in `src/StorageInventory.Library/StorageInventory.Library.csproj`, with exact versions and `PrivateAssets="compile"`. `No_package_references_anywhere` is **replaced** by the exact allow-list rule A-11, not deleted |
| Pinning | `RestorePackagesWithLockFile=true` with a committed `packages.lock.json` (content hashes), and `RestoreLockedMode=true` in CI and Publish (BLD-02). Lock files are RID-aware for `win-x64` (Q-12) |
| nuget.config | Package source mapping gains exactly the 4 IDs, as exact IDs (no `SQLitePCLRaw.*` wildcard). The remaining prefixes stay refused |
| Native | Loaded by the runtime from the extraction directory; verified as §5.6 |
| Licences | `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` as §5.5 |
| Size | The exe's growth is measured and recorded in `docs/release.md` (BLD-08) |
| Engine version | Exactly 3.53.3 asserted at run time; it covers `STRICT` (3.37), `WITHOUT ROWID` and `application_id` |
| Dependency boundary | The packages are referenced by `StorageInventory.Library` only; `StorageInventory.History` and Core stay package-free (D-41) |
| Future | Moving to 2.1.13, Microsoft.Data.Sqlite 11, SQLitePCLRaw 3 or `SourceGear.sqlite3` is a separate, reviewed change |

### 5.8 Engine configuration (D-37)

Every value is set by `LibraryDatabase` at open from constants and asserted by reading it back (A-22).

| Setting | Value | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `journal_mode` | **`TRUNCATE`** (a rollback journal), set by the first statement of every connection. If a hot journal exists, SQLite rolls it back while preparing that first statement, before the setting takes effect, in its default `DELETE` mode, and so deletes the journal ([E-8] 5; §6.3) | Read transactions write no file ([E-2], [E-8] 1), so the UI may read while a scan observes a source that contains the Library (INV-17). An appending import journals almost nothing (E-3). There is no `-wal` or `-shm` file, no checkpoint and no WAL growth (§5.9). After a commit or a rollback the journal is truncated to 0 bytes and kept ([E-8] 3), so no journal is created or deleted in normal operation |
| `synchronous` | **`FULL`** for every write transaction | With TRUNCATE, `FULL` syncs the truncated journal at commit, so a committed transaction cannot roll back after a power loss ([E-5] pager.c 2127–2136). Write transactions are few (attempt rows, one import, one deletion), so the cost is small |
| `locking_mode` | `NORMAL` | Readers and the one writer in the process coordinate through SQLite's file locks and the in-process gate (CONC-06) |
| `busy_timeout` | 5000 ms | A backstop only. The in-process gate (CONC-06) keeps our own readers from starving the writer; `SQLITE_BUSY` after the timeout is a snapshot failure, never retried in a loop |
| `temp_store` | `MEMORY` | No temporary file is ever created, inside or outside the Library. Its memory risk (REV-M01) comes from plain `VACUUM` and index builds, which 1.1.0 does not run (next row); ordinary queries are constrained by A-24 |
| `VACUUM`, `VACUUM INTO`, `ATTACH` | **Never run in 1.1.0** | Compaction and backup are 1.1.x (D-43). The first gate that adds them must re-review temporary storage and file creation (REV-M01, REV-M02) |
| `foreign_keys` | `ON` (also this build's default, [E-1]) | Referential integrity for catalogue and dictionary tables; observation-table integrity does not depend on it (§9.5, Q-07) |
| `trusted_schema` | `OFF` | Mitigates a crafted database that uses views or triggers to call functions (SEC-17) |
| Extensions | Never enabled (`EnableExtensions`, `LoadExtension` and `load_extension` never appear) | SEC-17, A-07 |
| `cache_size` | Import connection: 64 MiB (`-65536`); readers: the build default (2 MiB) | Bounds memory during the one-transaction import (PERF-11) |
| `application_id` | `0x53494E56` ("SINV") | Refuse to open files that are not Libraries (LIB-08) |
| `user_version` | The schema version (`1`) | Migrations (§9.6) |
| `STRICT` tables | Yes | Type safety for raw values |
| `secure_delete` | Not set (off) | Deleting a snapshot is logical: its bytes stay in free pages until reused. Settings says so in plain words (SEC-18, REV-O11) |

### 5.9 Why a rollback journal and not WAL

`c0c031f` chose WAL for concurrent readers. The repaired architecture (D-35) removes the case WAL was chosen for (a
writer that runs during traversal) and exposes three WAL costs that matter here:

| Concern | WAL | Rollback journal (`TRUNCATE`) |
|---|---|---|
| Does a read write a file? | **Yes**: every read transaction rewrote `-shm` [E-2]. Reading history while scanning `C:\` would write inside the source | **No** [E-2] |
| Side files and their life | `-wal` and `-shm` appear with the first connection and are checkpointed and deleted by the last close; `-wal` keeps its high-water size after PASSIVE checkpoints (review P4, P5) | `-journal`: 0 bytes at rest; non-empty only during a write transaction or after a crash (then it is hot and essential) |
| One-transaction import of a new snapshot | WAL grows to about the snapshot's size, then must be checkpointed [E-3] | Appended pages are not journalled; peak journal 0.0–0.1 MB for 1M rows [E-3, E-5] |
| Data at rest | Committed data can live only in `-wal` until checkpointed; a main-file-only copy loses it (review P1) | Committed data is always in the main file; with no hot journal, `library.sqlite3` alone is a complete copy |
| Readers during a write | Not blocked | Blocked while the writer holds `EXCLUSIVE` (after cache spill and at commit). Acceptable: the only long write is the post-scan import, during which the UI shows "Saving to History" and history pages wait (UI-10) |
| Network filesystems | Not supported | Not reliable either; both blocked (LIB-05a) |

Relative throughput of the two modes was the same in the probe [E-3]. **Windows confirmation** of the no-write
property and of import cost is TEST-W1 and Q-17/Q-21 (C4); a failure there stops C4.

---

## 6. Inventory Library model

The **Inventory Library** is StorageInventory's own persistent state: one SQLite database, the rollback journal SQLite
manages beside it, and one lock file, all in one directory. It is private data, because it holds file names, paths,
sizes and timestamps, exactly like the reports (§16).

### 6.1 Location and ownership

| ID | Rule | Status |
|---|---|---|
| LIB-01 | **Default and only location in v1.1:** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\StorageInventory\Library\`, resolved with `Environment.GetFolderPath(SpecialFolder.LocalApplicationData)` in exactly one App file (A-14). LocalAppData does not roam, and OneDrive's Known Folder Move covers Desktop, Documents and Pictures, not AppData *(scope not re-verified; LIB-05c blocks a sync root regardless)* | DECIDED (D-34) |
| LIB-02 | **Owned files, and nothing else** (§6.3). **Reports and spools never live in the Library** | DECIDED |
| LIB-03 | **Visibility:** the Home status area and Settings/About show the Library's full path, size and state. "Open Library folder" is added to the launch allow-list, runs only on an explicit click, and is disabled while an observation window is open (SEC-14) | DECIDED |
| LIB-04 | The Library code is **location-agnostic**: every Library API takes an explicit directory. Only the App resolves the default, so tests use `%TEMP%` fixtures | DECIDED |
| LIB-11 | A user-chosen or movable location and multiple Libraries are **deferred** (§4.2). They need persisted settings, which v1.1 does not add | DEFERRED |

### 6.2 Why a fixed location in v1.1

A user-chosen location needs a persisted pointer to it (a settings file) plus a safe move operation. Each is a new
write surface and a new failure mode: a dangling pointer; a Library on a sync folder or network share; half-moved
databases. The fixed location keeps v1.1's Library write boundary to one directory. The trade-offs are disk space on
the system drive (about 130 MB per 2M-file snapshot, measured on a proxy engine, §15) and the fact that the Library
lies inside `C:\`. The first is mitigated by REQ-11 and the size display; the second is acceptable **only** under
D-35 (§6.5), which is why D-34 is conditional on it.

### 6.3 Owned write inventory (LIB-02)

Every file StorageInventory, or SQLite on its behalf, can create, write, truncate, rename or delete in the Library
directory. Anything else found there is left untouched and never written. Every member is a direct child of the
validated Library directory; names are constants or built from constants plus a timestamp and a random suffix. Every
operation in this table, StorageInventory's or SQLite's, happens only inside a mutation lease of the Library activity
interlock (OBS-08), and so never during an observation window.

| File | Created by | Appears | Changes / disappears | Contents | After a crash | Cleanup owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `library.lock` | `LibraryStore` (`FileMode.CreateNew`, 0 bytes), always before any other member is checked, created or renamed | Library creation (LIB-07 step 3), or the first open or set-aside that finds the Library directory without one (LIB-08 step 2, LIB-13 step 2) | Never written, renamed or deleted. Held open with `FileShare.None` by the process that owns the Library, from its first acquisition until the process exits (CONC-01) | Nothing | Remains, 0 bytes; the OS releases the open handle when the process dies | Nobody (it persists) |
| `library.sqlite3` | `LibraryStore` (`FileMode.CreateNew`, 0 bytes); SQLite fills it | Library creation | Written only by write transactions (§10.3: T-CREATE, T0, T-IMPORT, T-OUTCOME, T-DELETE, T-RECOVER) and by SQLite's hot-journal rollback at open, each inside a mutation lease | All committed Library data | Consistent once its hot journal (if any) is rolled back | Only the user; StorageInventory renames it only in quarantine (LIB-13) |
| `library.sqlite3-journal` | **SQLite** (native) | The first write transaction of the Library (T-CREATE); again at the first write transaction after SQLite deleted it | **Normal operation:** holds original page images during a write transaction (non-empty only after the first page is journalled); truncated to 0 bytes and synced at commit and truncated at rollback (`TRUNCATE`, `FULL`, [E-5], [E-8] 3); otherwise persists as a 0-byte file. On Windows the writer keeps it open between its transactions ([G0 R4]); the writer exists only inside mutation leases (OBS-12). **Deleted by SQLite, never by StorageInventory, in exactly two cases:** (1) hot-journal recovery: the first statement of a new read-write connection rolls a hot journal back in SQLite's default `DELETE` mode, before that statement's `journal_mode=TRUNCATE` takes effect, and deletes the file ([E-4] 4, [E-8] 5); (2) a journal beside a **zero-page** main file is treated as a remnant and deleted ([E-5] `hasHotJournal`), which LIB-07 step 4 keeps from destroying evidence by refusing creation beside a non-empty journal. A read-only connection never deletes or writes it: facing a hot journal it fails with `SQLITE_READONLY_ROLLBACK` ([E-8] 4) | Page images of the current write transaction only | **Hot** (non-empty) if a write transaction was interrupted: essential for recovery; rolled back, and deleted, at the next open (LIB-08 step 5). Never separated from `library.sqlite3` (LIB-13) | SQLite (truncate; delete in cases 1 and 2); quarantine rename (LIB-13) |
| `library.damaged-<yyyyMMdd_HHmmss>-<6hex>.sqlite3` and the same stem with `-journal`, `-wal`, `-shm` | `LibraryStore.QuarantineSet` (`File.Move`, `overwrite: false`) | Only on the user's explicit "Set aside" action (LIB-13) | Never changed or opened again by StorageInventory | A set-aside Library and whichever members existed | Partial set possible (LIB-13 order) | Only the user |

**Never present, and never created by StorageInventory:** `library.sqlite3-wal`, `library.sqlite3-shm` (journal
mode is never WAL; their presence means another program opened the file, LIB-08 step 3), `-mj*` super-journals (no
`ATTACH`), `etilqs_*` temporary files (`temp_store=MEMORY`), backup or pre-migration copies (no `VACUUM INTO` in
1.1.0), and anything written by the scan pipeline (the spool lives in the report folder, §11.7).

**Cloud and network limitations.** SQLite's locking and journal guarantees assume a local filesystem; sync engines copy
files independently and create conflict copies. A Library path on a network location or inside a known sync root is
therefore blocked (LIB-05). The Library contains private data and is not uploaded by StorageInventory.

**Backups by the user.** When StorageInventory is closed and `library.sqlite3-journal` is absent (deleted by a recovery)
or 0 bytes,
`library.sqlite3` alone is a complete, consistent copy of the Library (rollback-journal mode keeps committed data in
the main file, §5.9). Settings says so. A non-empty journal means "open StorageInventory once to recover first".

### 6.4 Path restrictions

The Library path is validated with the same machinery as the report folder (`PathPolicy` normalisation, reparse-point
walk, canonical location) before every open and before every creation step.

| ID | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|
| LIB-05a | Library on a network location (UNC or mapped drive) | **BLOCKED**: SQLite's locking is not reliable over network filesystems |
| LIB-05b | A reparse point anywhere in the Library path (for example a profile relocated with a junction) | **BLOCKED**, as for report folders; Q-05 tracks the user-experience impact |
| LIB-05c | Library inside a OneDrive root (the environment variables `PathPolicy` uses) or another known sync root | **BLOCKED** in v1.1 |
| LIB-06a | The **source** equals, or lies inside, the **app-data root** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\StorageInventory\` (which contains the Library directory) | **BLOCKED** for every scan launched by the App, Save on or off. New `PathIssue` code `SourceInsideAppData` (INV-20). It is pure self-observation |
| LIB-06b | The **report folder** equals, or lies inside, the app-data root | **BLOCKED**. New code `OutputInsideAppData`: the directory is exclusively StorageInventory's |
| LIB-06c | The **Library lies inside the source** (for example the source is `C:\` or `C:\Users`) | **ALLOWED only under D-35** (§6.5). An informational preflight issue `LibraryInsideSource` says so |
| LIB-06d | The Library lies inside the report folder | Allowed (harmless: reports and spools are create-new, have different names, and are written only directly in the report folder) |
| LIB-06e | The canonical location of the source or of the Library cannot be determined | The Library is treated as **possibly inside** the source: D-35 applies (it applies to every scan anyway) and the snapshot records `library_inside_source = 2` (unknown) |

LIB-06a/b are checked by a Library-aware preflight layer in `StorageInventory.History` (`LibraryPathRules`), both
literally and by canonical location, and combined with v1's `PathPolicy` issues by the App. v1's Core `PathPolicy` is
unchanged, so C1 stays behaviour-preserving.

### 6.5 The Library inside a scanned source (D-35, replacing the rejected D-09)

Scanning `C:\` with the default Library is the common case. The rejected `c0c031f` rule (D-09) wrote staging rows into
the Library **during** the enumeration of `C:\`, so every snapshot of `C:\` recorded a timing-dependent partial image
of its own capture (review P4: 6,890 distinct WAL sizes during one capture), and "the scanned tree is read-only" became
false for the most common source (REV-B01). D-35 replaces it with these rules:

1. **Observation and Library mutation never overlap (INV-17, OBS-01 to OBS-13, D-51).** From before enumeration
   starts until the scan returns, nothing in the Library directory is created, written, truncated, renamed or deleted,
   no Library write transaction runs, and no maintenance runs; and no observation window opens while any such
   mutation is running. The Library activity interlock enforces both directions (§11.6). Reads are allowed, because in
   `TRUNCATE` mode a read writes no file ([E-2], [E-8]); Windows confirmation is Q-21.
2. **Capture data goes to an out-of-source spool (INV-21).** During enumeration the capture writes only to the
   observation spool, one create-new file in the validated **report folder** (§11.7). v1 already guarantees that the
   report folder is outside the source (literally and by canonical location) and backs that with the own-name tripwire;
   the spool's name is one of the run's own names.
3. **Import after the scan (IMP-01).** Only after the scan has returned `Finished` does the import read the spool and
   write the snapshot into the Library in one transaction, which is also the validity point.
4. **Observed, not excluded (LIB-14).** The traversal lists the Library directory like any folder: v1 reports and
   PowerShell parity are unchanged, and no new folder status exists. Because no mutation overlaps the window, the
   Library's contents do not change while it is observed, and this capture's own data appears only in later
   snapshots: a one-snapshot lag, explained in the UI's help. The sizes and times the scan **records** for the Library
   files are what the directory listing reports; on NTFS a listing may lag a file that another handle holds or
   recently held open (§8.1, REV-O06), as for any open file in v1. They are observations, not evidence of anything,
   and no detector uses them (OBS-04).
5. **Recorded and labelled.** The snapshot stores `library_inside_source` (0 no, 1 yes, 2 unknown) and the Library's
   root-relative path. Results, History and Compare label that folder "StorageInventory Library (app data)" (UI-07).
6. **Library-mutation tripwire (INV-19, OBS-04).** When the observation window opens and again when it closes, the
   capture records the interlock's mutation epoch and the database's commit generation (header change counter and
   page count, read from the file's contents). Any difference, or any interlock state other than this capture's
   Observing state in between, makes the capture `NotEligible (LibraryChangedDuringScan)`: reports stay valid,
   nothing is published. Directory-listing metadata is not used ([E-8]; G0-M04).
7. **App-data root blocked (INV-20, LIB-06a/b).**

**Same physical volume is fine.** A report folder outside the source but on the same disk (for example `C:\Reports`
while scanning `C:\Users\me\Pictures`) is acceptable: the invariant concerns mutation of the tree being observed, not
the physical device. If the source is `C:\` itself, every folder on `C:` is inside it, so v1 already requires the report
folder, and with it the spool, to be on another volume (`OutputInsideSource`).

**Alternatives considered** (review §12.3): writing during the scan (A1, rejected: B01); a Library outside every
source (A2, deferred to 1.1.x as a user choice: it needs settings and would make `C:\` unsaveable by default);
excluding the Library subtree (A3, rejected: it breaks "Complete" or makes every `C:\` scan Incomplete, and changes
parity); an in-memory spool (A5, deferred: O(files) memory); a staging SQLite database attached later (A6, rejected:
journals in the report folder and an `ATTACH` file capability); rewriting observed values at publish (A8, rejected:
falsifies observations).

### 6.6 Creation (LIB-07)

1. The Library is created **only** on explicit first use: the first scan whose "Save to History" option is on, at
   step P1 of §10.2 (inside that capture's Prepare lease, before the observation window), after preflight showed the
   Library path; or the explicit "Create a new, empty Library" action (Missing state, or after a set-aside), inside a
   Create lease (OBS-08). Creation never relies on an earlier "Not created" observation: every step below re-checks.
2. Path validation (§6.4). The directory chain is created with `Directory.CreateDirectory`, only in `LibraryStore`,
   and the directory is re-checked for being a reparse point.
3. **Writer lock first (CONC-01, G0-M06).** If this process already holds `library.lock`, continue. Otherwise open it
   with `FileMode.Open`, `FileAccess.Read`, `FileShare.None`; if it does not exist, create it with
   `FileMode.CreateNew`, `FileAccess.Write`, `FileShare.None` and keep that handle; if the create fails because the
   file now exists (another process won the race), open it once more as above. A sharing violation at any of these
   steps means **In use** (CONC-02): nothing else is checked, created or renamed. A second "not found" means
   Unavailable (the user can retry). Of two processes racing to create a Library, exactly one holds the lock and the
   other stops here (TEST-K1). No member of the Library is inspected before this step.
4. **Leftover check, under the lock:** creation is refused if `library.sqlite3-wal` or `library.sqlite3-shm` exists, or
   if `library.sqlite3-journal` exists and is non-empty (state Leftover files, §6.8). A 0-byte journal is harmless:
   SQLite treats it as not hot (and deletes it beside a zero-page main file, §6.3).
5. The directory is re-checked for being a reparse point; `library.sqlite3` is created with **`FileMode.CreateNew`**
   as a 0-byte file and closed. SQLite never uses a create mode (SEC-06, A-06).
6. The schema, `application_id`, `user_version` and `library_info` are written in **one** `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`
   transaction (T-CREATE) with `synchronous = FULL`. SQLite creates `library.sqlite3-journal` natively at this point.
   The writer connection is closed before the lease ends (OBS-12).
7. If step 6 fails, the main file stays 0 bytes or schema-less and SQLite rolls back its journal. A main file that is
   **0 bytes with no non-empty journal**, or that has `application_id = 0`, `user_version = 0` and no schema objects,
   is treated as uninitialised and initialised again on the next save.
8. Any other file at `library.sqlite3` is refused and never overwritten (§6.8).

### 6.7 Opening (LIB-08)

Every open (at App start, and on the user's "Retry") runs inside an **Open** mutation lease (OBS-08), so never
inside an observation window and never beside another mutation, and performs, in this order:

1. Path validation (§6.4).
2. If the Library directory does not exist → **Not created**, and the open ends: an open creates no directory.
   Otherwise acquire the writer lock (CONC-01) **before anything else in the directory is inspected**, exactly as
   LIB-07 step 3, creating `library.lock` if it is absent (the directory already exists, so an open adds at most this
   0-byte member, inside the Open lease) and remembering whether it existed before; a sharing violation → **In use**,
   and nothing else is inspected or opened (G0-M06).
3. **Member check, under the lock:** `library.sqlite3-wal` or `library.sqlite3-shm` present → **Not a Library
   (modified)**; main file absent or 0 bytes with a non-empty journal → **Leftover files**; main file absent otherwise
   → **Missing** if `library.lock` existed before this open, **Not created** if not.
4. **Header pre-check without SQLite** (`FileStream`, read-only, 100 bytes; A-03): magic `SQLite format 3\0`; header
   bytes 18 and 19 equal 1 (rollback-journal format; 2 means WAL → Not a Library); `application_id` at offset 68 equals
   `0x53494E56` (else **Not a Library**); `user_version` at offset 60 is supported (newer → **Incompatible**). SQLite
   never opens, and so never rolls back or writes, a file that fails this check (SEC-32).
5. Open the writer connection (`Mode=ReadWrite`, `Pooling=False`), set every §5.8 pragma, and assert the engine version
   and source id (§5.6). If a hot journal exists, preparing the first statement rolls it back and SQLite deletes the
   journal (§6.3 case 1, [E-8] 5).
6. Re-read `application_id` and `user_version`; compare the **schema fingerprint**: the rows of `sqlite_schema` exactly
   match the expected set for that version (no extra tables, views, triggers or indexes; SEC-17).
7. **Committed-state check:** `SELECT count(*) FROM snapshot WHERE state <> 2` must be 0 (SCH-08 #0). Anything else
   proves SQLite's atomic commit was defeated (a deleted or separated journal, [E-4]) → **Damaged** (D-47).
8. **Recovery (T-RECOVER, CONC-08):** attempts left `InProgress` by another session become `Interrupted`, and
   `library_info.last_opened_app_version` is set. Best effort: if it fails (for example the disk is full), the Library
   is **Available, recovery pending**: browsing works, saving is disabled until a later open succeeds (REV-M07).
9. Close the writer connection (OBS-12) and end the Open lease. Only now can a scan start (UI-13).

`quick_check` is not run at every open (2.5 s per about 4M rows, measured on the proxy engine, §15.1). It runs after an
SQLite error that reports corruption, to confirm the Damaged state.

### 6.8 Library states and failure handling (LIB-09)

| State | Detected by | Behaviour | Never |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Not created** | No Library directory; or no member files (apart from a `library.lock` this open had to create, LIB-08 step 2); or an uninitialised 0-byte main file (LIB-07 step 7) | Scans work as in v1. The Library is created on the first save (§6.6) | — |
| **Available** | Every LIB-08 check passes | Normal. "Recovery pending" (LIB-08 step 8) disables saving only | — |
| **Missing** | `library.lock` existed before this open but `library.sqlite3` does not, and no non-empty member remains (LIB-08 step 3) | "The Library was not found at <path>." Saving is disabled; an explicit "Create a new, empty Library" action | Recreate silently |
| **Leftover files** | A non-empty `library.sqlite3-journal` without a usable main file (absent or 0 bytes) | "Files from an interrupted Library remain at <path>." Saving is disabled; an explicit "Set aside the leftover files" action (LIB-13), after which a new Library can be created | Create a new database beside them (SQLite would delete the journal, [E-4]); delete them |
| **Unavailable** | Path blocked (§6.4), I/O error, access denied, or an unexpected SQLite engine version; or **restart required** after a class C failure during a Library mutation (the interlock is Faulted, OBS-13) | Explained with the path. Scans still run, without saving; when restart is required, scans are unavailable too and the message is "StorageInventory ran out of resources while changing the Library. Restart it." (UI-08) | Fall back to another location |
| **In use** | The writer lock is held by another process, in any logon session (CONC-02) | "The Library is open in another StorageInventory window. Scans here run without saving; a scan here may record the Library folder while the other window changes it." Scans still run, without saving; history, set-aside and creation are unavailable in this window (L-OBS1) | Open a second writer; read, write, rename or create any Library member from this process |
| **Incompatible** | `user_version` newer than supported (header pre-check) | "This Library was created by a newer StorageInventory (schema N). Update the app." No access at all | Downgrade or modify it |
| **Not a Library** | Wrong magic or `application_id`, WAL header, unexpected schema fingerprint, or `-wal`/`-shm` files present | "The file at <path> is not a StorageInventory Library or has been modified." Refused; "Set aside and start a new Library" offered | Overwrite, repair or delete it |
| **Damaged** | `SQLITE_CORRUPT` / `SQLITE_NOTADB` confirmed by `quick_check`, or committed snapshots not in state Published (LIB-08 step 7) | Saving disabled; published snapshots may be browsed read-only only if `quick_check` passes, and each is verified against its sealed totals before it is shown (D-47); "Set aside and start a new Library" | Delete it; recreate it silently; keep writing |

**LIB-13 (set aside a Library, the only rename).** On the user's explicit action only, and only through these steps,
in this order (G0-M06):

1. Acquire a **SetAside** mutation lease (OBS-08); refused, with its reason, while a scan observes or another mutation
   runs.
2. Hold the writer lock: if this process does not hold `library.lock`, acquire it exactly as LIB-07 step 3 (creating
   the lock file if it is absent). A sharing violation → **In use**: nothing is renamed. A process that does not hold
   the lock can never set aside, because no other code path renames Library members (A-04, A-25).
3. Close every Library connection of this process: the writer (already closed outside mutation leases, OBS-12) and
   every `Mode=ReadOnly` reader. Readers are stopped through the in-process gate (CONC-06): the lease takes the gate as
   a writer, in-flight reads are cancelled, reader connections are disposed, and Library pages show "Setting aside the
   Library…". SQLite then holds no handle on any member, so the renames cannot meet a sharing violation from this
   process ([G0 R4]).
4. Choose a quarantine stem `library.damaged-<yyyyMMdd_HHmmss>-<6hex>` for which none of the four target names
   (`.sqlite3`, `.sqlite3-journal`, `.sqlite3-wal`, `.sqlite3-shm`) exists; draw a new suffix up to 16 times, then
   fail as Unavailable.
5. Rename `library.sqlite3` **first** (if present), then each present member `-journal`, `-wal`, `-shm` to the same
   stem, each with `File.Move(overwrite: false)`, whose destination is created only if absent (it never replaces). A
   rename that fails stops the sequence; the state is re-derived (Leftover files if a non-empty journal remains) and
   shown.
6. The lock file stays and **stays held**: the process can then create a new Library (LIB-07) without releasing the
   lock, so no other process can slip in between set-aside and creation. End the lease.

Order matters: a journal left without its main file is never opened at the Library path (creation refuses non-empty
leftovers), whereas a main file left at the path without its hot journal would expose uncommitted data as valid
[E-4]. A crash between renames leaves the Leftover files state, whose action finishes the job under a new stem; the
help text says to keep every `library.damaged-*` file together. Set-aside files are never opened again by
StorageInventory. **"Keep a copy" is removed from 1.1.0** (set-aside already preserves every member; REV-H02).

### 6.9 What may delete or rename StorageInventory-owned data (LIB-12)

| Operation | Trigger | Scope | Safeguards |
|---|---|---|---|
| Delete a snapshot (REQ-11) | User, with confirmation | The rows of one **published** snapshot | One `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (T-DELETE, `FULL`) inside a **Delete** mutation lease: `UPDATE snapshot SET state = 3 WHERE snapshot_id = ? AND state = 2` must change exactly one row; delete that snapshot's `file_obs`, `folder_obs`, `scan_error` and `snapshot_extension_total` rows; delete the snapshot row; commit. Refused, with its reason, unless the interlock is Idle (OBS-07, OBS-11). Other snapshots are never touched. The attempt row stays |
| Record an attempt outcome | Automatic | One `scan_attempt` row of this session | `UPDATE … WHERE attempt_id = ? AND session_token = ? AND outcome = 1` |
| Recovery at open | Automatic, under the writer lock, inside the Open lease | `scan_attempt` rows with `outcome = InProgress` from other sessions | Only the outcome and end time change (CONC-08) |
| Set aside a Library (LIB-13) | User | The Library's member files | Renames only, `overwrite: false`; only under the writer lock and a SetAside lease, after every connection of the process is closed |
| **Never** | — | Deleting any Library file; purging rows of another capture; automatic retention; deleting on incompatibility or corruption; deleting reports or spools of past runs; `VACUUM`, backup or migration in 1.1.0 | — |

Unreferenced `name` and `folder_path` rows left by deletions stay until Compact (SREQ-09, 1.1.x). Their size is
counted in the Library size display.

### 6.10 Concurrency (LIB-10, D-36, replacing D-23)

`c0c031f` used a `Local\` named mutex, which excludes nothing across logon sessions, and verified the snapshot outside
the publish transaction, so a second session's open-time recovery could purge a live capture's rows between
verification and publication (REV-H03). D-36 enforces correctness in the store itself and uses the lock only to give
a clear "In use" state.

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| CONC-01 | **Writer lock.** The process that owns the Library holds `library.lock` open with `FileShare.None` from its first acquisition (at open, LIB-08 step 2, or at creation or set-aside, LIB-07 step 3, LIB-13 step 2) until it exits; it is never released and re-acquired in between. It is acquired **before** any SQL runs and before any Library member is inspected, created or renamed. File sharing modes are enforced by the filesystem for every opener, whatever its process, logon session or account, so the lock excludes other processes in other sessions too. No named kernel object is used (A-15) |
| CONC-02 | **Second instance.** A process that cannot acquire the lock shows **In use**, opens no connection to the database at all, never creates, renames or deletes a Library member, and runs scans without history. Different Windows accounts have different `%LOCALAPPDATA%` and so different Libraries; there is no cross-account sharing. **Limitation L-OBS1:** the interlock (OBS-07) is per process, so a scan run by the second instance can observe the Library while the owning instance mutates it. No snapshot is affected (only the owner publishes, and its own captures are protected by OBS-07 and INV-19); the second instance's reports may record a changing Library folder, as v1's reports can record another instance's report folder today. The In use message says so. An informational change note in the second instance's results is not adopted: it could only rest on listing metadata, which is unreliable while the owner holds files open (G0-M04) |
| CONC-03 | **One writer connection, only inside a mutation lease.** Only the lock holder opens a `Mode=ReadWrite` connection, and only while it holds a mutation lease (OBS-12); the connection is closed before the lease ends. Every write is a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE … COMMIT` transaction with `synchronous = FULL`. SQLite's own file locks (`RESERVED`, then `EXCLUSIVE` at spill and commit) exclude any other writer in any process while it runs |
| CONC-04 | **Publication is one transaction.** Import, verification (§11.8, IMP-05) and publication are one transaction (T-IMPORT). Nothing of the new snapshot is visible to any connection before its `COMMIT`; there is no committed intermediate state to purge, so no other process can make a partially imported snapshot valid. Verification cannot be interleaved with another writer because the transaction holds the database write lock |
| CONC-05 | **Readers.** UI pages use separate `Mode=ReadOnly` connections in the same process. SQLite isolation means they see only committed data. A read-only connection never rolls back a journal (recovery happens at open, LIB-08) |
| CONC-06 | **In-process gate.** `LibrarySession` owns an asynchronous reader/writer gate. A writer acquires it before `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`: new reads wait, and long in-flight reads (a comparison) are cancelled through `SqliteCommand.Cancel` and re-run by their page afterwards. `busy_timeout` is only a backstop |
| CONC-07 | **One capture at a time.** v1 already runs one scan at a time. A capture holds the interlock from its Prepare lease (T0) through its Observing state to the end of its Save lease (outcome recorded), with atomic hand-offs (OBS-10), so a second scan or capture cannot start until the first capture's outcome has been recorded (or its recording has failed, G0-O06) |
