| OBS-08 | **What a Library mutation is (exhaustive).** Any operation that can create, write, truncate, rename or delete a file in the Library directory, or that opens a read-write connection (which can roll a hot journal back and delete it): **Open** (LIB-08, with the lock acquisition, hot-journal rollback and T-RECOVER); **Create** (LIB-07 and T-CREATE: directory, lock file, main file); **Prepare(r)** (P1: creation if needed, then T0); **Save(r)** (P5 to P7: V, T-IMPORT, T-OUTCOME, closing the spool); **Delete** (T-DELETE); **SetAside** (LIB-13). A later operation that mutates the Library (migration and its backup, SCH-09; backup, SREQ-05; compaction, SREQ-09; deleting a source, SREQ-06; labels, SREQ-07; any repair) is a new lease kind, added here by the change that introduces it. It cannot be written any other way: every mutating API of `LibraryStore` and `LibraryDatabase`, and the writer connection itself, is reachable only through a `MutationLease` argument (A-25) |
| OBS-09 | **Primitive and ownership.** The interlock is an app-lifetime object inside `LibrarySession` (§14.2), created before the first page and present whether or not a Library exists or this process owns it. Its state is one field guarded by a private monitor; every operation is a non-blocking *try* that holds the monitor only to test and change the state, never across I/O or an `await`. A grant returns a lease token (`MutationLease`, `ObservationLease`) that only the interlock can construct; disposing it, always in `finally`, ends the lease. Hand-offs are methods on the lease that change the state in one monitor section. The state, with a reason naming the operation in progress, is published as an observable value that the UI binds to (UI-13). There is no queue and no waiting for a lease. Lock order: interlock (state change only), then the in-process gate (CONC-06), then SQLite |
| OBS-10 | **Capture hand-offs and the next scan.** A capture goes Prepare(r) → Observing(r) at P2 (after the writer has been closed) → Save(r) at P4 → Idle at the end of P7. Save(r) ends only after T-IMPORT has committed or rolled back, T-OUTCOME has been written or has failed (best effort, bounded by `busy_timeout`), the writer has been closed and the spool handle has been closed. **The next scan becomes eligible to start exactly when the interlock is Idle again.** So the next capture's T0 can never precede the previous capture's recorded outcome, and no scan can start while a capture is saving (G0-B01 failure modes 1 and 2). A Save-off scan goes Idle → Observing(r) → Idle. A capture whose creation or T0 failed continues as a Save-off scan: its Prepare lease hands over to Observing(r), and at P4 the interlock returns to Idle |
| OBS-11 | **Refusal, cancellation and failure.** A refused request is an outcome, not an error: a refused scan starts nothing (no report-folder file, no attempt, O28); a refused mutation changes nothing (O29); each carries the reason of the operation that holds the interlock. Cancellation never skips a transition: **Stop scan** cancels the scan token (the scan returns Cancelled; the P4 hand-off follows; Save(r) records the outcome). **Stop saving** cancels the save token: V or T-IMPORT stops and rolls back within PERF-16, T-OUTCOME records SaveCancelled, and the lease ends normally. Cancelling a deletion rolls T-DELETE back. An Open or a SetAside cannot be cancelled once started (SQLite's hot-journal rollback; at most four renames), so closing the window waits for it. **If the import fails,** the transaction rolls back, T-OUTCOME records SnapshotFailed (best effort), the spool is closed and the lease ends; the next scan is then eligible, and Save to History is unavailable only if the Library's new state requires it (O24). "Stop saving and scan" is not offered as one action: the user stops saving, then starts the scan once the interlock is Idle |
| OBS-12 | **The writer exists only inside a mutation lease.** The `Mode=ReadWrite` connection is opened after a mutation lease is granted and closed before it ends (CONC-03). So during an observation window no StorageInventory handle with write access is open on any Library member; `library.lock` stays held (it is never written) and reader connections may be open. Rollback mode has no close-time side effect ([E-2]); reopening costs one open, the §5.8 pragmas and the engine assertion |
| OBS-13 | **Faulted.** A class C exception (CAT-03) while a mutation lease is held leaves the interlock Faulted: no lease of either kind is granted again in this process, the Library state becomes Unavailable ("restart required") and scans are unavailable ("Restart StorageInventory", CAT-04, O30). A class C exception during an observation (no mutation in progress) ends the observation as v1 does (O18) |

**Machine-checkable form.** The interlock is a small state machine that performs no I/O itself, so TEST-W2 checks
every (state, request) pair against the table above, runs randomised concurrent request sequences from several threads
and asserts the invariant after every step, and uses test hooks that pause inside every mutation kind to show that an
observation request is refused while the mutation runs and granted only after it ends. A-25 proves statically that no
mutation can bypass it.

### 11.7 The observation spool (SPOOL-01 to SPOOL-24, D-35, D-49, D-50)

| ID | Aspect | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| SPOOL-01 | **Location** | Directly inside the validated **report folder** of the run, never elsewhere. This was evaluated, not assumed: (a) v1 validates the report folder to be outside the source literally and by canonical location, with the own-name tripwire as backstop; (b) the user chose it and it already receives the same class of private data (the Files report lists every path); (c) create-new, own-name and three-proof rules already govern it; (d) reports are mandatory in 1.1.0 (D-43), so it always exists; (e) every other candidate is either possibly inside the source (`%TEMP%` lies inside `C:\`) or is the Library, which must not be written. Costs: transient space in the report folder; spool traffic when the report folder is on a network share; possible upload when it is inside OneDrive (both already warned about by v1, and the warnings now name the spool). It is the 1.1 design, not a permanent one: a reviewed change may replace it (for example a user-chosen working folder with SREQ-04 in 1.2). **Inherited residual (G0-O10):** when v1's canonical check is unavailable, v1 only warns (`PathPolicy.cs:189-195`) and its tripwire fires only after a report, or now the spool, already exists inside the source. The spool adds no new class of exposure; TEST-SP2 keeps the alias case |
| SPOOL-02 | **Owner** | The scan's `ReportRun` (Core) creates, holds and deletes it. The capture receives the live in-memory `ObservationSpool` handle, never a path |
| SPOOL-03 | **Name** | `History_<runId>.spool.tmp`. It is added to the run's own names **only when a capture is requested**, so `ReportRun.Prepare`'s existence check and the scan's tripwire cover it; runs without a capture keep v1's six names exactly |
| SPOOL-04 | **Create-new** | `FileMode.CreateNew`, `FileAccess.ReadWrite`, `FileShare.None`, `FileOptions.DeleteOnClose`, by `ReportRun` only, after the report-folder reparse-point re-check, when the run creates its first report file. One file per capture |
| SPOOL-05 | **Privacy** | It holds names, sizes, timestamps and attributes in a machine-readable private binary format, the same class of data as the Files report. It is held exclusively (no other process can open it), deleted when closed, and never uploaded by StorageInventory |
| SPOOL-06 | **Never a snapshot by itself** | (1) No API accepts a spool path; the importer takes only the live handle of the current capture. (2) Its header carries the capture token, which must equal the in-memory token and the attempt's `capture_token`. (3) Its trailer must be present and match the scan result's totals. (4) The SHA-256 in the trailer must match the file's physical byte sequence, verified by the pass V (SPOOL-23) before any record is used. (5) Import happens only after a `Finished` result and P5. (6) A spool left by another process is never opened, because no code finds spools by name. (7) The format is private and versioned, never an export (the INV-11 analogue) |
| SPOOL-07 | **Lifecycle** | Created at scan start; file and error records appended during enumeration; finalised folder records written after aggregation, before v1's Folders report (SINK-03 #4); the run index, trailer and digest written at the seal, `OnScanEnded(Finished)`, after v1's reports; read back through the same handle, first by the verification pass V in physical order at P5 (SPOOL-23), then by the importer in key order (IMP-03); closed after T-IMPORT commits, or at any earlier stop. It is **append-only**: no byte is ever rewritten or patched after it is written |
| SPOOL-08 | Cancellation | Closed, and so deleted, at the end of the cancelled scan or the cancelled import |
| SPOOL-09 | Failed scan | Closed and deleted. Unlike v1's report files, which v1 lists as incomplete artefacts, the spool has no value to the user and its ownership is proven by the exclusive handle held since creation. It never appears in `StorageScanResult.IncompleteArtifacts`: it no longer exists when a Cancelled or Failed result is returned |
| SPOOL-10 | Report failure | The scan is not Finished, so there is no import (LIFE-08); closed and deleted |
| SPOOL-11 | **Spool write failure** | The isolated observer is faulted (class A) and immediately disposes its buffer and closes the handle, which deletes the file and frees its space for the reports; the capture becomes `NotEligible (SpoolWriteFailed)`. The same happens when the spool gives way to the reports (SPOOL-24) |
| SPOOL-12 | **Seal and digest** | The writer feeds **every byte it writes, in write order**, to an incremental SHA-256; since the file is append-only, write order is physical order. At `OnScanEnded(Finished)` it writes the run index and the trailer fields (SPOOL-20), then the 32-byte digest of **every byte before the digest** (header, records, folder section, run index and trailer fields; bytes `[0, L − 32)` of a file of length `L`), then flushes. The digest is the file's last 32 bytes; nothing follows it. No `fsync`: the spool is never read after a crash. P5 checks that the trailer's counts and totals equal `StorageScanResult.Totals` (files, folders, bytes, scan errors, reparse points skipped, file reparse points) |
| SPOOL-13 | Database-import failure | T-IMPORT rolls back; the spool is closed and deleted; the reports stay valid |
| SPOOL-14 | **Deletion mechanism** | `DeleteOnClose`: the OS deletes the file when its last handle closes. The three v1 proofs hold by construction (created by this run with `CreateNew`, directly in the validated folder, with the exact own name, held exclusively ever since). `DeleteOnClose` appears only in `ReportRun` (A-02) |
| SPOOL-15 | **App crash, process termination** | The OS closes the handle and deletes the file (documented Win32 behaviour of delete-on-close handles; Q-20 confirms it with antivirus, SMB and OneDrive report folders) |
| SPOOL-16 | Disk full | A write fails → SPOOL-11. Projected size about 80 B per file (PERF-13, measured in C5, Q-18), below the Files report of the same scan. The spool yields space to the reports before they run out (SPOOL-24) |
| SPOOL-17 | **Stale spool** (power loss or OS crash only) | The file remains as `History_<runId>.spool.tmp`. It is **never imported** and **never deleted automatically**: after a restart nothing proves ownership, and v1 has no delete-by-name. The Interrupted attempt's message names the report folder and file as plain text (never a link, SEC-15) and says it may be deleted |
| SPOOL-18 | Cleanup summary | Automatic deletion happens only by closing the delete-on-close handle this process created. Never by name, never for another run's file |
| SPOOL-19 | Finished but incomplete scans | The spool carries every finalised folder record with its status and every error record; the snapshot is published as Incomplete with its completeness evidence (LIFE-09) |
| SPOOL-20 | **Format 1 (one file)** | Little-endian. **Header:** magic `SISPOOL1`, format `1` (u16), flags (u16), capture token (16 bytes), run ID (u16 length + UTF-16), `scanner_contract` (u16), created UTC ticks (i64). **Records** (tag u8 + payload): `0x01` file: folder index (i32), name (u16 length + exact UTF-16 code units), size (i64), created, modified and accessed ticks (i64 each, `i64.MinValue` = null), attributes (i32); the sequence is the record's position among file records. `0x02` error: type (u8), path (u32 length + UTF-16, as v1 reports it), message (u32 length + UTF-16). `0x03` finalised folder: index and parent index (i32), name (UTF-16), status, status reason (0xFF = none) and `subtree_complete` (u8 each), attributes (i32), created and modified ticks, direct and total bytes, files and subfolders (i64 ×6), largest file bytes (i64 or null). **Run index:** per file run in emission order, (folder index i32, offset i64, count i32). **Trailer:** tag `0x7F`, counts, totals, offsets of the folder section and run index, then the SHA-256 digest (32 bytes) of every preceding byte of the file (SPOOL-12). **Order:** header, then file and error records in emission order, then the folder section, then the run index, then the trailer; nothing is written out of this order |
| SPOOL-21 | **Memory bounds** | Writer: one 1 MiB buffer, the incremental SHA-256 state, and the run index (16 B per folder: about 7.5 MB at 470k folders; INV-13's "grows with the folder count" is unchanged in kind). Reader and importer: IMP-08 |
| SPOOL-22 | **Performance expectations** | Projected, not measured: sequential appends of about 80 B per file add at most 10% to v1's enumeration time (PERF-02a). C5 measures on the 250k tree and on 1M and 2M generated trees (Q-18) |
| SPOOL-23 | **Verification pass V (D-50)** | At P5, before **any** record is used, one sequential read of the whole file from offset 0 through the live handle, in physical order: (1) the length `L` is at least header plus trailer, and SHA-256 of bytes `[0, L − 32)` equals the last 32 bytes; (2) the header: magic, format 1, and the capture token equal to the in-memory token; (3) the record region, parsed record by record up to the folder-section offset: every tag is `0x01` or `0x02`, every length is within bounds, and the counts per tag equal the trailer's; (4) the file runs are rebuilt from the record stream (a maximal sequence of file records with one folder index, errors allowed between them; no folder index may start two runs) and must equal the stored run index entry for entry; (5) the folder section: the trailer's count, ascending indexes, `parentIndex < index`, and totals recomputed from it equal the trailer's and `StorageScanResult.Totals`; (6) the trailer's tag and offsets are consistent with the positions found. Any failure → `NotEligible (SpoolInvalid)` (O10). Only after V passes may the importer seek by the run index and read in key order (IMP-03). The spool is held exclusively and never written after sealing, so those reads return the bytes V verified; silent corruption by the storage stack between the two reads is outside the integrity model, as for every file StorageInventory reads. V costs one extra sequential read and IMP-03's error pass another over the record region, both usually from the OS cache and both part of PERF-14 |
| SPOOL-24 | **The spool gives way to the reports (G0-M07)** | Every 16 MiB it writes, when the first finalised folder arrives, and after the last finalised folder (that is, after its folder section and **before** v1 writes the Folders report and, in sorted mode, the sorted Files report, SINK-03 #4), the spool writer reads the report volume's free space (`GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` on the report folder, already in the native set, §7.8). If free space is below the **reserve**, the larger of 512 MiB and three times the spool's current size, the spool faults itself (SPOOL-11), which releases all its space for the reports; the capture becomes `NotEligible (SpoolWriteFailed)` with the reason "the report folder's drive is nearly full". Basis: after the folder section, v1 writes the Folders report and, in sorted mode, the whole sorted Files report while the spool writes only its small tail (run index and trailer, at `OnScanEnded`), and the Files report is projected at more than twice the spool's size per file; C5 confirms or revises the factor with measured sizes (Q-18). **Residual, documented:** a report write can still fail first if the reports need more than the reserve after the last check; the reserve makes that need a near-full volume. Preflight shows the projected spool size ("Saving needs about N MB in the report folder while scanning") when an earlier snapshot of the source gives a file count |

### 11.8 The post-scan import (IMP-01 to IMP-10)

| ID | Rule |
|---|---|
| IMP-01 | Runs only after P5 holds, inside the capture's Save lease: the scan returned `Finished`, the spool is sealed, passed V (SPOOL-23) and matches, and both tripwires (INV-19, ID-10) passed |
| IMP-02 | One transaction, T-IMPORT: the writer connection is opened inside the Save lease (OBS-12), acquires the in-process gate (CONC-06), then `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`; `synchronous = FULL`; import `cache_size` 64 MiB |
| IMP-03 | **Key order.** (1) Resolve or create the volume and source rows from the P0 decision, re-reading them inside the transaction (`SourceChanged` if a chosen row is gone). (2) Insert the snapshot row (`state = 1`, sealed totals) with `snapshot_id = next_snapshot_id`. (3) Read the folder section; intern folder paths in ascending discovery index (parents first) to build the folder-index → `path_id` map; insert `folder_obs`. (4) Sort the run index by `path_id`; for each run, seek to it, read its file records, intern their names, sort the run by `name_id` and insert `file_obs` with `seq`. A run longer than 262,144 files is inserted in spool order unsorted (correct, only slower). (5) Stream error records into `scan_error` with root-relative paths, in one more sequential pass over the record region that skips file records. (6) Insert `snapshot_extension_total` from totals accumulated while reading. Key order was 2.3 to 2.9 times faster than shuffled order in the probe [E-3] |
| IMP-04 | **Name cache per capture** (REV-M06): created when T-IMPORT begins and discarded when it ends, committed or rolled back, so a rolled-back `name_id` can never be reused. Bounded LRU of 262,144 entries backed by `name` lookups inside the transaction |
| IMP-05 | **Verification inside the transaction, before the final statements:** (a) V passed at P5 for this spool handle (SPOOL-23; the digest is never recomputed from key-ordered reads); (b) the numbers of rows inserted equal the trailer and `StorageScanResult.Totals`; (c) the §9.5 invariants 1 to 12 hold for this `snapshot_id` (aggregate SQL); (d) the attempt row exists with this session's token, this capture's token and outcome `InProgress`. Any failure → `ROLLBACK`, `SnapshotFailed (InvariantViolation)` |
| IMP-06 | **Final statements**, each required to change exactly one row: `UPDATE snapshot SET state = 2, published_utc = ? WHERE snapshot_id = ? AND state = 1`; `UPDATE scan_attempt SET outcome = 2, source_id = ?, ended_utc = ? WHERE attempt_id = ? AND session_token = ? AND capture_token = ? AND outcome = 1`; `UPDATE library_info SET next_snapshot_id = next_snapshot_id + 1 WHERE next_snapshot_id = ?`. Then `COMMIT`, the validity point |
| IMP-07 | Cancellation: CAN-01d |
| IMP-08 | **Memory:** page cache 64 MiB; the folder map (8 B per folder); one run buffer (at most 262,144 records); the name cache; the extension totals. Projected peak ≤ v1 peak + 300 MB at 2M files (PERF-11b) |
| IMP-09 | **Performance:** projected from measured rates (about 190k rows/s on the proxy engine, §15.1; about 300k rows/s key-ordered in rollback mode on Linux, [E-3]): 10 to 15 s for 2M files. [E-3] measured one `file_obs`-shaped table only; the real import also inserts into `name` and `folder_path`, whose `UNIQUE` indexes modify existing pages once the Library holds other snapshots or sources, and those pages are journalled (G0-O03). Budget PERF-14; Windows measurement Q-17 with the full schema (TEST-P1) |
| IMP-10 | After `COMMIT`: release the gate, close the writer, close the spool (deleted), end the Save lease (OBS-10), refresh the UI |

### 11.9 Results and API compatibility (SINK-07)

- `IStorageInventoryScanner`, `StorageScanOptions` and `StorageScanResult` are **unchanged and public**. v1 callers
  and tests compile and behave identically; `ScanAsync`/`Scan` run the same internal pipeline without a capture.
- `InventoryScanner` gains two **internal** methods: `PrepareCapture(StorageScanOptions) → PreparedCapture` (validates
  exactly as `Scan` does and names the run; creates nothing) and `RunCapture(PreparedCapture, IProgress<StorageScanProgress>?, CancellationToken) → CaptureScanOutcome { StorageScanResult Scan; ObservationSpool? Spool; SpoolFault? Fault; }`.
- **In `StorageInventory.Library`** (the SQLite adapter, D-41): `LibrarySession`, `LibraryDatabase`, `LibraryStore`,
  `SnapshotImporter`, the catalogue and the row streams, and the `InventoryRun` orchestrator that performs P0 to P7.
  Its result is `InventoryRunResult { StorageScanResult Scan; SnapshotCaptureResult Snapshot; }`, with
  `SnapshotCaptureResult.Outcome` one of `NotRequested | Published | NotEligible | Failed | SaveCancelled | Declined`,
  plus `SnapshotId?`, `SourceId?`, `Failure?` (a `CaptureFailureKind` and message) and `Timings`. The scan result is
  delivered to the App as soon as the scan returns, before saving starts (UI-10).
- **In `StorageInventory.History`** (package-free, D-41): identity matching, the Library path rules, the comparison
  engine and its oracle, history arithmetic, and the row-stream interfaces the Library implements.
- `ScanPhase` gains the appended value `SavingToHistory`, reported by `InventoryRun`, never by the v1 scanner.
  Appending is compatible, and the v1 UI maps unknown phases safely. TEST-O5 (Core's public surface equals v1's) is
  re-baselined once, in C5, to allow exactly this value (G0-O07).
- **The PowerShell reference** keeps its authority over v1 traversal and report semantics. **Persistence is not
  reimplemented in PowerShell** (D-19). Parity tests keep running on the refactored scanner (C1).

---

## 12. Diff semantics and truth table

This section makes INV-16 mechanical. The `c0c031f` definition proved absence by **exact** path, which is unsound on
Windows' case-insensitive directories (REV-B02; [E-6]: 2,482 to 2,728 unsound verdicts per 20,000 generated worlds).
The repaired definition (D-38) aligns folders first, then proves absence with a comparison key only where the
source's name matching is verified to be contained in that key; everywhere else, absence needs a proof that does not
depend on name matching at all, or the result is Unknown (NAME-03, NAME-04, NAME-09).

### 12.1 Scope and preconditions (DIFF-01, DIFF-10)

- **Only published snapshots of the same source are compared:** `Compare(PublishedSnapshotId older, PublishedSnapshotId newer)`,
  with `older.source_id == newer.source_id`. Comparing across sources is refused (ID-11).
- **"Older" and "newer" follow capture order** (`snapshot_id`, SCH-11), never the wall clock (REV-M04). The API
  normalises the order and states it in the result. If the snapshots' `finished_utc` order disagrees with capture
  order, the result carries a **clock anomaly** flag and the UI shows both dates (D-44).
- Comparing a snapshot with itself is valid (DIFF-P5).
- **DIFF-10 (input verification, D-47).** While streaming each snapshot, the engine counts folders and files and sums
  bytes, and compares them with that snapshot's sealed totals. Any mismatch aborts the comparison with "The stored data
  of this snapshot is inconsistent" and no partial result; the Library is flagged for verification. A comparison can
  therefore never present Missing or Added computed from rows that a defeated commit left behind (LIFE-11).
- The identity banner of ID-09 applies. For sources with basis `Location` (network) or `UserAsserted`, Missing reads
  "missing at this location".

### 12.2 Alignment and the case-aware absence proof (DIFF-11, DIFF-12)

**Observations.** For a published snapshot `S`: every observed item has an exact path (a sequence of exact names,
NAME-01) and a type (file or folder); every observed folder `f` has a status. `Listed_S(f)` ⇔ `f` is present in `S`
and `status(S, f) = Ok`: its listing was complete. `Partial`, `Unreadable` and `ReparsePointSkipped` are **not**
Listed. `K` is the comparison key (NAME-03). `A` is the source's matching assurance, `Verified` or `Unverified`
(NAME-04); both snapshots belong to one source and so share it. `Dom(x, f)` holds when the name `x` and every child of
`f` of `x`'s type are in the verified domain (NAME-09).

**DIFF-11 Alignment** (a relation between folders of O and folders of N, built top-down):

1. The roots are aligned (same source, identity re-verified at capture, ID-10).
2. For each aligned pair `(a_O, a_N)`, children are matched in two steps:
   1. **Exact:** an O child and an N child with exactly the same name and the same type are matched.
   2. **Case-fold (DIFF-07):** only if **both** `a_O` and `a_N` are Listed and, when `A = Verified`, `Dom(c, a_O)` and
      `Dom(c', a_N)` hold. An unmatched O child `c` and an unmatched N child `c'` of the same type with
      `K(c) = K(c')` are matched if `c` is the **only** child of `a_O` of that type with that key and `c'` is the
      **only** child of `a_N` of that type with that key, **counting matched children**.
3. Matched folder pairs are aligned (and the rule recurses); matched file pairs are compared (§12.5). Nothing else is
   aligned. An aligned pair is **exactly aligned** when every step from the roots to it was an exact match (step 2.1).

**DIFF-12 Proven absence.** Let `p` be an **unmatched** O item. Let `(a_O, a_N)` be the aligned pair of `p`'s deepest
aligned proper ancestor, let `c` be the child of `a_O` on the path to `p` (`c = p` when `p` is a direct child), and let
`T` be `c`'s type (a folder when `p` lies strictly below `c`). Define

- `Cand_N(a_N, c, T)` = the children of `a_N` of type `T` with `K(x) = K(c)`, **matched or not**;
- `Kids_N(a_N, T)` = all children of `a_N` of type `T`, matched or not;
- **`A = Verified`:** `ProvenAbsent_N(p)` ⇔ `Listed_N(a_N)` **and** `Dom(c, a_N)` **and** `Cand_N(a_N, c, T) = ∅`;
- **`A = Unverified`:** `ProvenAbsent_N(p)` ⇔ `Listed_N(a_N)` **and** `(a_O, a_N)` is exactly aligned **and**
  `Kids_N(a_N, T) = ∅`. No name comparison is involved: nothing of type `T` exists anywhere in the listing.

`p` is **Missing** iff `ProvenAbsent_N(p)`; otherwise it is **Unknown** with side `Newer` and the first reason that
applies:

| Condition | Unknown reason |
|---|---|
| `a_N` is Unreadable | `AncestorUnreadable` |
| `a_N` is Partial | `ListingIncomplete` |
| `a_N` is ReparsePointSkipped | `BehindLink` |
| `a_N` is Listed, `A = Verified`, and `Dom(c, a_N)` fails | `NameMatchingUnverified` (a name uses a character newer than the pinned Unicode version) |
| `a_N` is Listed, `A = Verified`, and `Cand_N` is not empty | `NameVariantPresent` (another child has the same name apart from letter case or Unicode encoding) |
| `a_N` is Listed, `A = Unverified`, and `Kids_N(a_N, T)` is not empty or the pair is not exactly aligned | `NameMatchingUnverified` (this drive's rules for matching names are not verified, so another item here could be the same one) |

Symmetrically, an unmatched N item `q` is **Added** iff `ProvenAbsent_O(q)` (the same definition with O and N
exchanged), otherwise **Unknown** with side `Older` and the reason taken from `a_O`. A Missing item whose `a_N` has a child of the other type with key `K(c)` carries the
informational sub-reason `ReplacedByFolder` / `ReplacedByFile`; Added likewise `ReplacedFile` / `ReplacedFolder`.

**Soundness argument (why Missing is proof).** *Unverified sources.* An exact match is the true position in any
directory under any name matching (below), so an exactly aligned `a_N` is the true position of `a_O`; if `a_N` was
listed completely and holds no child of type `T` at all, nothing of type `T` was at `c`'s position, whatever the
filesystem treats as the same name. No premise about name matching is used. *Verified sources.* The premise is that
the filesystem's name matching is contained in `K` for names in the verified domain (NAME-04, NAME-09); every step
below that relies on `K` also requires `Dom`, so the premise is used only where it holds. By induction down the
alignment, each aligned N folder is either the true position of its O folder, or that O folder's true position is
**absent** in N:

- An exact match is always the true position: in any directory, the entry whose exact name is `c` is the only entry of
  `c`'s equivalence class.
- A case-fold match happens only under two complete listings, and only when `c'` is the sole same-type child of
  `a_N` with key `K(c)`. Any entry at `c`'s position in N would have a key equal to `K(c)` and type `T`, so it is
  either `c'` (then `c'` is the true position) or does not exist (then `c` and everything below it are truly absent,
  and any Missing claimed below it is correct).

Now take a Missing `p` whose aligned `a_N` is the true position of `a_O`. `a_N` was listed completely, and no child of
`a_N` of type `T` has key `K(c)`; any entry at `c`'s position would have one. So nothing of type `T` exists at `c`'s
position, and `p`, which is `c` or lies below it, did not exist in N when `a_N` was listed. **If `a_N` is not Listed,
or a same-type child with key `K(c)` exists (matched or not), absence proves nothing, and the result is Unknown.** The
argument needs no knowledge of per-directory case sensitivity, of changes to it between scans, or of the exact
filesystem fold, only that `K` is at least as wide. (A scan is not an instantaneous snapshot: Missing means "absent
when its parent folder was listed", REV-O12; the help text says so.) [E-6] tests **the rule** on a toy model (a fold of
ASCII letters, a key wider by one character): 0 unsound verdicts in 100,000 generated worlds with a key wider than the
filesystem's and 100,000 with an equal key, against 2,482 to 2,728 per 20,000 worlds for the exact-name key. It is
evidence about the rule, not about `K` (G0-O09); `K`'s width is NAME-04's per-unit condition ([E-9]) and Q-14's census.

**Why not the review's proposed rule.** The review's proposal (its §10.6) treats two listed case variants as proof that the directory
was case-sensitive, and lets a variant already matched to another item fail to block the proof. Both fail when the key
is wider than the filesystem's fold or when case sensitivity changed between scans ([E-6]: 45 to 71 unsound verdicts per
20,000 worlds). An earlier draft of this repair that counted only *unmatched* variants for the case-fold match also
failed on a targeted case ([E-6], T41). Rule 2.2's "counting matched children" closes it.

### 12.3 File classification (DIFF-02)

`O` is the older snapshot, `N` the newer.

| O | N | Class | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| file | file (exact or case-fold match) | **Changed** if the metadata differs (DIFF-04), **Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate)** under DIFF-05, else **Unchanged** | Case-fold matches carry the flag `NameSpelling` |
| file | unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing** | "Last seen": O (REQ-16) |
| file | unmatched, **not** `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Unknown** (side Newer, reason from §12.2) | **Never Missing** |
| file | a folder at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing** (`ReplacedByFolder`) | Proven: N observed a folder there and, under §12.2's rule for the source's assurance, no file that could be the same; otherwise the row above (Unknown) applies |
| unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_O` | file | **Added** | |
| unmatched, **not** `ProvenAbsent_O` | file | **Unknown** (side Older, reason from §12.2) | Present now, but we can't say it is new |
| a folder at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_O` | file | **Added** (`ReplacedFolder`) | Otherwise Unknown (side Older) |

### 12.4 Folder classification: two axes (DIFF-03, D-39)

Folders get an **existence class** and, when Present, a separate **content certainty**. Only the existence class takes
part in the headline counts and in the properties; content certainty is reported beside it and is **never counted as
Unknown** (REV-H05).

| O | N | Existence class |
|---|---|---|
| folder | folder (aligned) | **Present** |
| folder | unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing**. All of its O descendants are Missing too (same anchor, same candidate set) |
| folder | unmatched, not proven | **Unknown** (with the reason); all O descendants Unknown with the same reason |
| unmatched, `ProvenAbsent_O` | folder | **Added** (all N descendants Added) |
| unmatched, not proven | folder | **Unknown** (side Older); its N descendants Unknown |
| folder | a file at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_N` | **Missing** (`ReplacedByFile`); descendants Missing. Otherwise Unknown, as the third row |
| a file at the same exact name, and `ProvenAbsent_O` | folder | the folder is **Added** (`ReplacedFile`); all N descendants Added. Otherwise Unknown (side Older), as the fifth row |

The content certainty of a Present folder uses `total_bytes`, `total_files` and `total_subfolders`:

| Certainty | Condition | Shown as |
|---|---|---|
| **Exact** | Both sides have `subtree_complete = 1` and neither side is a link | The delta. Content "changed" if non-zero, "unchanged" if zero |
| **NotComparable** | Either side has `subtree_complete = 0` (and neither is a link) | "Not comparable (incomplete)", with both values. Both are lower bounds, so their difference is not a bound in either direction |
| **NotObserved** | Either side has status `ReparsePointSkipped` | "Link (not followed)"; links are never entered, so there is no content on that side |

**Bytes.** File bytes are exact. The Missing (Added) byte total of a Missing (Added) folder is labelled "at least"
when its subtree was incomplete on its own side (REV-O07).

### 12.5 What "Changed" means for files (DIFF-04, DIFF-05, D-13, D-40)

"Changed" and "Unchanged" compare the metadata **the directory listing reported** (REV-O06); the help text says so.

| Field | Effect |
|---|---|
| `size_bytes` differs | **Changed** (flag `Size`) |
| `modified_utc` differs (`NULL ≠ value`; `NULL = NULL`) | **Changed** (flag `Modified`), except under DIFF-05 |
| `created_utc` differs | Stays **Unchanged**, informational flag `Created` |
| `attributes` differ | Stays **Unchanged**, informational flag `Attributes` (the Archive bit changes on every backup, for example) |
| Only the name's spelling differs (a case-fold match, DIFF-07) | Stays **Unchanged**, informational flag `NameSpelling` |
| `accessed_utc` differs | **Ignored entirely.** Last-access updates are lazy, often disabled, and coarse |

**DIFF-05 (clock shifts on FAT-family volumes, D-40).** FAT and FAT32 store local times, and exFAT written by devices
without its UTC-offset field behaves the same way; a time-zone or daylight-saving change shifts the reported UTC time
without any content change. **Rule:** on sources whose `fs_type` is `FAT`, `FAT32` or `exFAT`, a matched file whose size
is equal and whose `modified_utc` differs by a non-zero whole multiple of 15 minutes within ±14 hours is **Unknown
(ChangeIndeterminate, PossibleClockShift)**: counted separately, never Unchanged, never Changed (REV-M05). A size
difference makes it Changed as usual. exFAT stays in the rule until Q-03 proves otherwise on real media.

### 12.6 Reparse points (DIFF-06)

| Situation | Result |
|---|---|
| Link folder in both O and N | Existence **Present**, content **NotObserved**. Shown as "Link (not followed)". No descendants on either side |
| A normal folder in O is a link folder in N | The folder is Present (aligned), content NotObserved. Its O descendants have `a_N` = the link folder, which is not Listed, so they are **Unknown (Newer, BehindLink)**, **never Missing** |
| A link folder in O is a normal folder in N | Its N descendants are **Unknown (Older, BehindLink)** |
| File reparse points (for example symbolic-link files) | Ordinary file observations (their listed size), compared like any file |

### 12.7 Spelling-only renames (DIFF-07)

A case-fold match (§12.2 rule 2.2) treats two names that differ only in letter case or Unicode encoding as the same
item, **only** under two complete listings and only when each is the sole same-type member of its key class on its
side. Matched folders align their subtrees. In every other case exact-name identity applies, and the unmatched items are
classified by §12.2 (typically `NameVariantPresent`).

**Limitation L-DIFF1.** A case-fold match is a claim that both names occupy the same namespace position. In a folder
that distinguishes the two spellings (a case-sensitive NTFS folder, or NFC and NFD forms on NTFS), where one name was
deleted and the other created between the scans, StorageInventory reports a spelling change instead of Missing and
Added. This can only under-report, never produce Missing or Added, and the help text says so. Collecting per-directory
case sensitivity (Q-16, 1.2) would narrow it.

**Limitation L-DIFF2.** On sources whose matching assurance is `Unverified` (every network share, UDF, unknown
filesystems, and any class before its Q-14 census passes), a deletion is reported as Missing only when its folder,
reached through exact matches, now holds nothing of that type; otherwise it is Unknown (`NameMatchingUnverified`), and
additions likewise. This under-reports by design (INV-16); the Compare banner says so for such sources ("This drive's
rules for matching names aren't verified; items that may have been deleted are shown as Unknown").

### 12.8 Algorithm (DIFF-08, bounded memory)

1. **Folder alignment (O(folders) memory).** Load `(path_id, parent, name_id, discovery_index, status, subtree_complete, totals)`
   for both snapshots (at most about 470k rows each on the reference drive). Exact alignment is equality of `path_id`
   (paths are interned per source with exact names). Apply the case-fold step to unmatched child folders of aligned
   Listed pairs, decoding names and computing `K` and `Dom` only for folders that have unmatched siblings; record for
   each aligned pair whether it is exactly aligned. Verify folder counts against the sealed totals (DIFF-10).
2. **File merge (streaming).** Read `file_obs` for both snapshots ordered by `(folder_path_id, name_id)` (the primary
   key; measured 3.3 s for 2 × 2M on the proxy engine). Exactly aligned folders share `path_id` and merge in one pass;
   case-fold-aligned folders are read with keyed seeks. Within one folder pair, if any file is unmatched and both
   folders are Listed, decode that folder pair's names, compute `K` and `Dom`, apply the case-fold step and the proof
   rule for the source's assurance.
   Memory for this step is bounded by the largest single folder (PERF-12).
3. **Unmatched subtrees** stream with the verdict of their topmost unmatched folder (all its descendants share its
   anchor, candidate set and so its verdict).
4. **Output.** A summary (count and bytes per class and reason, flags, folder existence counts, content-certainty
   counts); top-N heaps (largest Added, largest Changed by absolute size delta, largest Missing, largest Unknown); an
   in-memory list of non-Unchanged items capped at PERF-06, beyond which the UI shows the summary and top-N lists; the
   folder-growth list (Exact deltas only). Streamed counts and bytes are checked against the sealed totals (DIFF-10).
   Comparison results are **not persisted** (D-14).

### 12.9 Truth table (DIFF-09)

"Complete" and "Incomplete" describe the whole snapshot; the per-location rules above do the work. Each row is a
required test case (TEST-D1). Unknown is written as Unknown (side, reason). Rows are for a `Verified` source unless
they say otherwise.

| # | Scenario | Older | Newer | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | complete → complete, file unchanged | file | same file | Unchanged |
| T2 | size changed | file | size differs | Changed {Size} |
| T3 | only mtime changed | file | mtime differs | Changed {Modified} |
| T4 | attributes only | file | attributes differ | Unchanged {Attributes} |
| T5 | created time only | file | created differs | Unchanged {Created} |
| T6 | last-access only | file | accessed differs | Unchanged (no flag) |
| T7 | invalid timestamp on one side | mtime NULL | mtime value | Changed {Modified} |
| T8 | file deleted, parent fully listed in N, no name variant | file | parent Ok, no child | **Missing** |
| T9 | the file's folder is Unreadable in N | file | parent Unreadable | **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T10 | the folder listing stopped part-way in N | file | parent Partial, file absent | **Unknown (Newer, ListingIncomplete)** |
| T11 | unreadable **ancestor** in N (grandparent Unreadable, so the parent is absent) | file | anchor = grandparent, Unreadable | **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T12 | file in a folder that was Unreadable in O | (folder Unreadable) | file present | **Unknown (Older, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T13 | new file under a folder Listed in O | parent Ok, no child | file | **Added** |
| T14 | different unreadable folders on each side | mixed | mixed | per location: T8 to T13 apply independently |
| T15 | folder disappears from a fully listed parent | folder + files | parent Ok, no folder, no variant | folder **Missing**, all descendants **Missing** |
| T16 | folder disappears, parent Partial in N | folder + files | parent Partial | folder and descendants **Unknown (Newer, ListingIncomplete)** |
| T17 | folder became a junction in N | folder + files | link folder | folder Present (content NotObserved); descendants **Unknown (Newer, BehindLink)** |
| T18 | junction became a real folder | link folder | folder + files | folder Present; descendants **Unknown (Older, BehindLink)** |
| T19 | link folder in both | link folder | link folder | Present, content NotObserved; no descendants on either side |
| T20 | file replaced by a folder with the same name | file | folder | file **Missing (ReplacedByFolder)**; folder **Added (ReplacedFile)** |
| T21 | folder replaced by a file | folder + files | file | folder and descendants **Missing (ReplacedByFile)**; file **Added (ReplacedFolder)** |
| T22 | case-only rename, both parents Listed, singleton key classes | `Report.txt` | `report.txt` | Unchanged {NameSpelling} (Changed if the size or mtime also differs) |
| T23 | a case variant appears beside the old name (case-sensitive folder in N) | `a.txt` | `a.txt`, `A.txt` | `a.txt` Unchanged; `A.txt` **Unknown (Older, NameVariantPresent)** (was "Added" in `c0c031f`: in O, `a.txt` may have been the same position) |
| T24 | source root unavailable at the newer scan | — | no snapshot (the scan Failed: SourceMissing) | Nothing to compare. History shows a ScanFailed attempt |
| T25 | whole volume offline | — | no new snapshot possible | Nothing to compare. Earlier snapshots remain comparable with each other |
| T26 | root Unreadable in N (the scan finished, Incomplete) | files | root Unreadable | **every** O item Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable); no Missing |
| T27 | moved file (path A → path B), both folders Listed | file at A | file at B | A **Missing**, B **Added** (no "Moved" in 1.1, FID-01) |
| T28 | FAT32 source, same size, mtime +1 h | file | +3600 s | **Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate, PossibleClockShift)** (was Unchanged in `c0c031f`) |
| T29 | the same snapshot compared with itself, any completeness, links and unreadable folders included | S | S | all files Unchanged; all folders Present; zero Added, Missing and Unknown; content certainty reported as it is (never counted as Unknown) |
| T30 | folder content delta, both subtrees complete | 10 GB | 12 GB | folder Present, content **Exact**, +2 GB |
| T31 | folder content delta, one side incomplete | 10 GB | 9 GB (incomplete) | folder Present, content **NotComparable**, both values shown; not Unknown |
| T32 | hard link: two paths to the same file | 2 paths | 1 path removed, parent Listed | the removed path Missing; the other Unchanged (FID-03) |
| T33 | case-renamed folder, Unreadable in N (review CE-1) | `A\Photos\x.jpg`, all Ok | `A` Ok lists `photos`; `A\photos` Unreadable | `Photos` Present {NameSpelling}; `x.jpg` **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T34 | folder Unreadable in O, case-renamed in N (review CE-2) | `A` Ok lists `backup`, Unreadable | `A\Backup\y.txt` | `y.txt` **Unknown (Older, AncestorUnreadable)** |
| T35 | case-renamed folder is a junction in N (review CE-3) | `A\Backup\…` | `A\backup` junction | descendants **Unknown (Newer, BehindLink)** |
| T36 | folder replaced by a file whose name differs only in case | `A\b\x` | `A` Ok lists file `B` only | `A\b` and `x` **Missing (ReplacedByFile)**; `B` **Added (ReplacedFolder)** |
| T37 | self-comparison of a snapshot with a junction and an Unreadable folder | S | S | files all Unchanged; folders all Present; Unknown 0; content NotObserved and NotComparable reported separately |
| T38 | identity evidence differs between the window's opening and closing | — | — | not published (`NotEligible (IdentityChangedDuringScan)`); nothing to compare |
| T39 | `finished_utc` order is the reverse of capture order | — | — | ordered by capture sequence; clock anomaly flagged |
| T40 | case-sensitive folder loses one of two variants | `Makefile`, `makefile` | `makefile` (Listed) | `makefile` Unchanged; `Makefile` **Unknown (Newer, NameVariantPresent)** |
| T41 | case sensitivity changed and a wider-key variant appears ([E-6] targeted case) | case-sensitive `{s\child, S}` | case-insensitive `{S\child, ſ}` | `S` Present (exact); `s` and `s\child` **Unknown (Newer, NameVariantPresent)**; `ſ` **Unknown (Older, NameVariantPresent)**; `S\child` **Added** (O's `S` was listed complete and empty) |
| T42 | case-only rename under a Partial parent | `A` Ok lists `Report.txt` | `A` Partial lists `report.txt` | no case-fold match; `Report.txt` **Unknown (Newer, ListingIncomplete)**; `report.txt` **Unknown (Older, NameVariantPresent)** |
| T43 | NFC name in O, NFD name in N, singleton classes | `café.txt` (NFC) | `café.txt` (NFD) | Unchanged {NameSpelling} (L-DIFF1) |
| T44 | names with different unpaired surrogates, parent Listed | `a\uD800` | `a\uDC00` | different keys: `a\uD800` **Missing**, `a\uDC00` **Added**; each round-trips exactly (NAME-01) |
| T45 | FAT-family offsets | same size on FAT32, mtime +5:30; on exFAT −3:00; on FAT32 +0:07; on FAT32 size differs and +1 h | — | Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate); Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate); Changed {Modified}; Changed {Size, Modified} |
| T46 | a snapshot's rows disagree with its sealed totals | — | — | the comparison is refused ("stored data inconsistent"); no partial result |
| T47 | network source (basis Location, `Unverified`): a file deleted from a folder that still holds other files | file | file absent, parent Ok, other files present | **Unknown (Newer, NameMatchingUnverified)** (was Missing in `456b233`), with the location banner and the L-DIFF2 note |
| T48 | Missing folder whose older subtree was incomplete | folder (subtree incomplete) | parent Ok, no folder | folder Missing; Missing bytes labelled "at least" |
| T49 | combining-mark reordering under a per-unit fold that maps U+0345 to U+0399 (a model fold in the test; not the NTFS or exFAT default tables, [G0 R2]) | `A\<α U+0345 U+0301>\x.jpg`, all Ok | `A` Ok lists `<α U+0399 U+0301>` only; that folder Unreadable | the folders are case-fold matched (equal `K`, NAME-03); `x.jpg` **Unknown (Newer, AncestorUnreadable)**. Under the `456b233` key the folders did not match and `x.jpg` was Missing |
| T50 | network source (`Unverified`): the last file of a folder deleted; the folder reached through exact matches | `\\nas\m\A\x.txt` | `A` Ok, holds folders but no file | `x.txt` **Missing**, "missing at this location" (no name comparison is needed) |
| T51 | network source (`Unverified`): a folder case-renamed and emptied of files | `A\Docs\x.txt` | `A` Ok lists `docs`, Ok, no file | `Docs` Present {NameSpelling}; `x.txt` **Unknown (Newer, NameMatchingUnverified)** (`docs` is not exactly aligned) |
| T52 | `Verified` source: a sibling's name uses a character not assigned in Unicode 16.0.0 | `A\x.txt` | `A` Ok lists `<name with an unassigned code point>` only | `x.txt` **Unknown (Newer, NameMatchingUnverified)**; the new name **Unknown (Older, NameMatchingUnverified)** |
| T53 | `Verified` source before and after: the same pair of snapshots compared as `Unverified` (assurance lowered) | any | any | no Missing or Added that was not also reported under `Verified`; every item whose class changes becomes Unknown (DIFF-P11) |

### 12.10 Properties (DIFF-P1 to DIFF-P12)

Checked by property-based tests over generated trees (TEST-D2, TEST-D3, TEST-D5):

- **DIFF-P1 Partition:** every file observed in O or N gets exactly one file class; every folder gets exactly one
  existence class.
- **DIFF-P2 Soundness:** Missing ⇒ `ProvenAbsent_N` and Added ⇒ `ProvenAbsent_O`, exactly as defined in §12.2, checked by
  an independent checker over the engine's output.
- **DIFF-P3 Monotonicity:** if `N'` is `N` made less observable (a folder's status lowered from Ok to Partial or
  Unreadable with its unlisted children removed, or a folder turned into a link), then
  `Missing(O, N') ⊆ Missing(O, N)` and `Added(O, N') ⊆ Added(O, N)`, and every file whose class changes becomes Unknown.
  Symmetrically for `O`. Content certainty is outside this property (it may move from Exact to NotComparable).
- **DIFF-P4 Complete snapshots:** if both snapshots are Complete, every Unknown has reason `BehindLink` (under a folder
  that is a link on exactly one side), `NameVariantPresent`, `NameMatchingUnverified`, or `ChangeIndeterminate`.
- **DIFF-P5 Self-comparison:** `Compare(S, S)` has all files Unchanged, all folders Present, and zero Added, Missing and
  Unknown, for every S.
- **DIFF-P6 Duality:** `Added(O→N)` equals `Missing(N→O)` as path sets; Changed and Unchanged are symmetric; Unknown
  swaps its side. Tested through an internal entry point that does not normalise the order.
- **DIFF-P7 Conservation:** `|O files| = Unchanged + Changed + Missing + Unknown_O` and
  `|N files| = Unchanged + Changed + Added + Unknown_N`, where `Unknown_O` counts Unknown items observed in O (an
  Unknown (ChangeIndeterminate) item is observed in both).
- **DIFF-P8 Location independence:** changing the mount point or drive letter between the snapshots changes no result.
- **DIFF-P9 Oracle agreement:** on generated trees of up to 2,000 items, the engine equals the reference oracle
  (§12.11) item by item.
- **DIFF-P10 World soundness:** on generated *worlds* (per-directory case sensitivity, changes to it, renames, links,
  partial and unreadable listings, multi-code-point names), the engine emits no Missing whose position is occupied or
  unknowable in the newer world, and no such Added in the older world (§12.11). For `Verified` worlds the world's fold
  is a random per-unit fold contained in `R`; for `Unverified` worlds it is an **arbitrary** equivalence on names (full
  case folding, normalisation, random merges), so soundness there must not depend on `K` at all.
- **DIFF-P11 Key and assurance monotonicity:** replacing `K` by a wider key, or lowering the assurance from `Verified`
  to `Unverified`, never increases the Missing or Added counts.
- **DIFF-P12 Input verification:** a snapshot whose rows disagree with its sealed totals is never compared (T46).

### 12.11 Reference oracle and world-model test (DIFF-13)

- **The oracle** is a brute-force implementation written independently of the engine: it shares only the
  observation types, the `K` function and the `Dom` predicate. It enumerates both snapshots as sets of `(exact path, type, status)`,
  computes the alignment of §12.2 literally with nested loops, and classifies every item from the definitions. TEST-D2
  runs it against the engine on at least 10,000 generated cases per property, with trees of up to 2,000 items that
  include: random Partial, Unreadable and link statuses on either side; case variants in case-sensitive folders; case
  renames; NFC and NFD forms; multi-code-point sequences (combining marks in both orders, the U+0345 family, Hangul
  syllables and jamo); unpaired surrogates; characters outside the verified domain; file and folder replacements;
  case-renamed folders that become links or unreadable; and both assurance levels.
- **The world-model test** (TEST-D5) generalises [E-6]: it generates ground-truth worlds with per-directory case
  sensitivity and changes to it, observes them as a v1 scan would, runs the **production engine**, and counts unsound
  Missing and Added verdicts. Acceptance: **zero** in at least 100,000 worlds in the default configuration and 100,000
  in the biased configuration (names restricted to one key class plus a filler, up to 20 changes), each run for both
  assurance levels as DIFF-P10 describes. It also runs two **controls that must fail**: the engine with an exact-name
  key, and the engine with the `456b233` key `R(NFD(n))` in `Verified` worlds whose fold maps U+0345 to U+0399; each
  must produce unsound verdicts, proving the test can detect both defects.
- **`K` itself** (TEST-D6) is table-driven and tested three ways: the per-unit condition over every code unit for each
  fold table Q-14 checks; the property "names equal under a random per-unit fold contained in `R` have equal `K`"
  ([E-9] 3); and the Q-14 census data, single code units and multi-code-point sequences, where every set of names the
  filesystem treated as one must lie within one `K` class.

---

## 13. History, analytics and search

Everything in this section is **metadata arithmetic over published snapshots**. There is no content analysis (§4.1).
"Older" and "newer" always mean capture order (SCH-11).

### 13.1 Totals over time (HIST-01, REQ-09, SREQ-11)

- **Series:** bytes, files and folders per published snapshot of one source, from the snapshot's sealed totals, in
  capture order.
- **Table (MUST, REQ-09):** one row per snapshot, with attempts inline as rows with no values.
- **Chart (SHOULD, SREQ-11):** drawn from the same data; the chart never joins complete and incomplete points into one
  line without marking the difference.
- **Incomplete snapshots** are labelled **"at least"** (lower bounds) in both.
- **Capacity and free space** at each scan are a separate column/series. They are the volume's, not the source's (for a
  subfolder source that matters).
- **Accessibility:** every chart has its equivalent table (UI-09).

### 13.2 Growth (HIST-02, REQ-10, SREQ-02)

- **Folder growth** between two snapshots comes from the §12.4 content axis of Present folders.
  - Top-N folders by absolute **Exact** delta, split into grew and shrank.
  - A separate count of folders whose content is **Not comparable (incomplete)** or **Not observed (link)**, with a
    way to list them.
  - **Never** rank a folder on a non-Exact delta.
- **"Fastest-growing over a period"** is the Exact delta between the first and last published snapshots in the period.
  A per-day figure may be shown as delta ÷ elapsed time, labelled "average between two scans", not a measured rate.
- **File-count growth** works the same way, using `total_files`.
- **Extension and category growth** (SREQ-02) uses `snapshot_extension_total`, with categories from v1's
  `FileTypeCatalog`. An incomplete snapshot's per-extension totals are lower bounds and are marked as such.
- **Bytes added between scans** use the comparison summary (Added and Changed bytes; "at least" where §12.4 says so).
- **Largest additions and changes** use the comparison's top-N lists (REQ-07).

### 13.3 Last seen (HIST-03, REQ-16)

- **MUST:** in a comparison, every **Missing** item shows "last seen: <older snapshot date>". That is exact: the older
  snapshot observed it.
- "Last seen anywhere in history" (SREQ-10) is 1.1.x. Its rule is fixed now: it searches older published snapshots of
  the source, newest first, stops at the first observation, and when it passes a snapshot where the path could not be
  proven absent it says "possibly present later (not observable on <date>)" rather than claiming a gap.

### 13.4 Offline browsing (HIST-04, REQ-08, D-48)

`LibraryExplorer` serves the v1 results tabs from a published snapshot, with the same rows in the same order as v1's
`ReportExplorer` over the CSV reports of the same run. The stored discovery ordinals reproduce v1's tie order exactly
(REV-M09):

| Tab | Query | v1 rule it reproduces |
|---|---|---|
| Overview (largest top-level folders) | `folder_obs` at depth 1, `ORDER BY total_bytes DESC, discovery_index ASC LIMIT 100` | `ReportWriters.FolderOrder` then depth 1 |
| Largest folders | `ORDER BY total_bytes DESC, discovery_index ASC LIMIT 2000` (`ExplorationViewModel.FolderLimit`) | Folders report order |
| Largest files | `ORDER BY size_bytes DESC, seq ASC LIMIT 1000`, a bounded top-N sort (A-24); measured 0.43 s for 2M files without an extra index | Sorted Files report order; the unsorted-report heap also breaks ties by sequence |
| File types | `snapshot_extension_total` plus `FileTypeCatalog` | Files report aggregation |
| Errors | `scan_error` in `seq` order, with counts by type | ScanErrors report order |

**Parity test (TEST-H2):** for every fixture scan with Save to History on, in both sorted and unsorted Files mode,
`ReportExplorer` over the CSVs and `LibraryExplorer` over the snapshot return the same rows in the same order, including
fixtures with many equal-size files and empty folders. The only allowed differences are documented presentation
differences (local-time rendering; the CSV's U+FFFD rendering of an unpaired surrogate, which the Library keeps exact).

**When the source is offline,** browsing works from the Library alone. v1.1 has one action that needs the live
filesystem, opening the run's report files if they still exist, through the v1 allow-list; it is unavailable with its
reason otherwise.

### 13.5 Search (SRCH-01, SRCH-02, SREQ-01): a filter model, not a language

**SRCH-01 (semantics):**

| Filter | Semantics |
|---|---|
| Source; snapshot (default: latest published) | Exactly one snapshot per search in v1.1. Cross-history search is deferred |
| Name contains | Case-insensitive (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`) substring match on the decoded exact name (NAME-06) |
| Path contains | Case-insensitive substring match on the rebuilt folder path |
| Extension | Exact match on `ExtensionOf(name)`, case-insensitive |
| Size range | Inclusive, in bytes. The UI accepts KB, MB and GB and converts with 1024 units, as v1 does |
| Modified between | UTC bounds converted from local dates. Items with `NULL` modified times are excluded, with the count shown |
| Status relative to a reference snapshot | **Deferred** (it needs a stored comparison) |

**SRCH-02 (execution):**
1. Match names in .NET over the name dictionary (about 250k distinct names for 2M files; Unicode-correct).
2. Match folder paths in .NET over the loaded folder table.
3. Pass the matching IDs as one bound parameter read with `json_each(?)` (SQLite's built-in JSON table function), so the
   read-only connection needs no temporary table.
4. Filter `file_obs` for the snapshot by those IDs and the numeric bounds.

Results are paged, and each page is bounded (PERF-04). There is **no free-text query language and no SQL typed by
users** (SEC-04).

### 13.6 Age cohorts (SREQ-03)

- **Cohorts:** "Not modified in 1 / 2 / 5 / 10 years, according to the file's modified date", counting files and
  bytes.
- **Mandatory wording:** "Modified dates are set by the programs that write files. Copying, restoring or syncing can
  keep old dates or set new ones, so a file's modified date does not prove when it was last used."
- **Created and last-access dates are never used for cohorts.**

### 13.7 When a volume is offline (HIST-05)

- All history, comparison, browsing and search features work from the Library alone.
- The source card shows **Offline** (after an on-demand check) or **Not checked**, and "last seen <date> as <mount
  point>".
- **Scan is unavailable**, with the reason.
- **Online state is never cached as truth:** it is re-checked on demand (ID-07).

---

## 14. WPF information architecture

### 14.1 Shell and navigation (UI-01)

v1's single window with three stages becomes a **shell window with a navigation list** (keyboard-accessible, with
automation names) and a content region. One view per page, one view model per page, and a small navigation service.
There are no UI packages (A-11).

| Page | Purpose | Main content | Empty state |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Home** | Where am I? | Library status and path (LIB-03); the most recent snapshot per source; attempts needing attention (interrupted captures, attempts of sources never created, stale-spool notes); a "Scan a drive or folder" button | "No history yet. Scan a drive or folder and save it to start a history." |
| **Sources** | Every drive and folder ever saved | A card per source: name, filesystem, identity confidence and "How is this drive recognised?", **Online / Offline / Not checked**, last seen, latest totals and completeness, number of snapshots, Library space used; a "Check drives" action | "No sources yet." |
| **Scan** | The v1 flow, unchanged | Setup (with preflight), Progress, Results, reusing v1's views. The preflight gains one line: "Save to History: <source> in <Library path>", or the reason it can't. Progress gains the "Saving to History" phase, and the Results view shows it until saving ends (UI-10, UI-13) | — |
| **History** | One source over time | The timeline table (REQ-09) and chart (SREQ-11); attempts inline; per-snapshot actions: Browse, Compare with…, Delete (with confirmation; available only while the interlock is Idle, UI-13) | "No saved scans for this source." |
| **Compare** | What changed? | Selectors (§14.4); a summary by class and reason; tabs for Added, Changed, Missing, Unknown and folder growth; top-N lists; flags | "Pick a source with at least two saved scans." |
| **Browse** | A snapshot, offline or online | The v1 results tabs, served from the Library (§13.4) | — |
| **Search** (if SREQ-01 ships) | Find items in one snapshot | The filter form (§13.5) and paged results | "Enter at least one filter." |
| **Settings & About** | Transparency | Library path, size and state; version; schema version; SQLite version and source id; licences; privacy wording (deleted snapshots stay in free space until reused; how to copy the Library while the app is closed) | — |

### 14.2 App-lifetime state and services (UI-12, REV-M10)

Long-lived state is owned by services created once per app session, not by page view models:

| Service | Owns | Lifetime and rules |
|---|---|---|
| `ScanSession` | The running scan and capture: options, cancellation (scan and save tokens, CAN-02), progress, the `StorageScanResult` (delivered as soon as the scan returns), the save outcome, the class C boundary (CAT-04). It starts a scan only with an `ObservationLease` or a Prepare lease from the interlock, and it is the only caller of the scanner in the App (A-25) | App lifetime. **C2 moves the scan out of `MainViewModel` into `ScanSession`**, so C8 adds pages without rework |
| `LibrarySession` | The writer lock, connections (the writer only inside mutation leases), the in-process gate, the **Library activity interlock** (OBS-07 to OBS-13) with its state and reason, and the Library **state as an observable value** (§6.8) | App lifetime, created before the first page, present even when no Library exists. Every page reacts to state changes (for example Available → Damaged mid-session) and to interlock changes (UI-13) |
| `ICatalogue` | Published snapshots and attempts; the only constructor of `PublishedSnapshotId` | Stateless over `LibrarySession` |
| `ICompareService` | A running comparison and its result (up to PERF-06's cap) | Owned by the Compare page; cancelled and released when the user navigates away |
| `ISourceStatus` | On-demand online checks (ID-07) | Stateless; never probes without a user action |

Pages depend on these interfaces, so their view models are unit-tested with fakes; a test factory creates
`PublishedSnapshotId` values for page tests.

### 14.3 Scanning, saving and Library operations: phases and commands (UI-02, UI-10, UI-13)

- **UI-02:** a running scan keeps going when the user moves to another page. The navigation list shows "Scanning…"
  or "Saving to History…" while either runs.
- **UI-10 (saving phase):** after the scan returns, the results are shown at once (the reports are valid). Until the
  capture's Save lease ends, the Results view shows a **"Saving to History"** status band with the saving progress and
  its own **Stop saving** button (CAN-02), and **New scan is unavailable** with the words "Available when saving to
  History has finished (or stop saving)". The Results view never looks idle while saving runs: the band stays until
  the outcome is shown ("Saved to History", or the reason it was not saved). History pages that need the Library
  during saving show "Saving to History…" and refresh when it ends.
- **Closing the window** during a scan uses v1's confirm-and-wait flow (`MainWindow.OnClosing`). During saving it asks:
  "Saving to History. **Stop saving and close** / **Keep saving**." Stopping rolls back (O4, O27). During a deletion
  it asks "Deleting a snapshot. **Stop and close** / **Keep deleting**" (stopping rolls T-DELETE back). During an
  Open or a set-aside it waits, showing the operation's name (OBS-11). When Faulted it closes at once.
- **UI-13 (phases and command availability).** The App's workflow phase is derived from two observable values only:
  the interlock state (OBS-07) and whether `ScanSession` has a scan or its v1 workbook step running. Every command's
  availability, and the reason shown when it is unavailable, comes from this table (TEST-U2 checks each cell):

| Phase (shown as) | Interlock | Steps | Start or New scan | Stop scan | Stop saving | Delete snapshot | Set aside, Create new Library, Retry open | Browse, Compare, Search, History | Open Library folder |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Opening the Library** | Mutating(Open) | LIB-08 | No: "Opening the Library…" | — | — | No | No | Wait ("Opening the Library…") | No |
| **Ready** (Idle) | Idle | — | **Yes** (Save to History per §6.8 and ID-13) | — | — | Yes | Yes, per Library state | Yes | Yes |
| **Starting scan** | Mutating(Prepare r) | P1 | No: "A scan is starting" | Yes (the scan returns Cancelled at P2, O3) | — | No | No | Yes (reads wait briefly for T0) | No |
| **Scanning** (then "Writing reports") | Observing(r) | P2 to P4 | No: "A scan is running" | Yes | — | No: "Unavailable while scanning" | No: "Unavailable while scanning" | Yes (reads only) | No (OBS-05) |
| **Results, saving to History** | Mutating(Save r) | P5 to P7 | **No**: "Available when saving to History has finished (or stop saving)" | — | **Yes** | No | No | Library pages wait ("Saving to History…"); the Results tabs (CSV reports) work | Yes |
| **Results** (saved or not; Idle) | Idle | after P7, or a Save-off scan | **Yes** | — | — | Yes | Yes, per Library state | Yes | Yes |
| **Deleting a snapshot** | Mutating(Delete) | T-DELETE | No: "Deleting a snapshot…" | — | — | No (Cancel deletion: Yes) | No | Library pages wait | Yes |
| **Creating or setting aside the Library** | Mutating(Create) or Mutating(SetAside) | LIB-07, LIB-13 | No: "Setting up the Library…" | — | — | No | No | No | No |
| **Restart required** | Faulted | — | No: "Restart StorageInventory" | — | — | No | No | No | No |

  A command that is enabled can still be refused if the state changes between the click and the request; the
  refusal carries the same words and nothing starts (OBS-11, O28, O29). The v1 workbook step, which runs after a scan
  in the report folder, keeps v1's own availability rule and is neither a Library mutation nor an observation.

### 14.4 Compare selectors (UI-03)

- The source picker lists only sources with at least two published snapshots.
- The **Older** and **Newer** pickers list only that source's **published** snapshots in capture order, never attempts.
  The default is the latest two, and there is a **Swap** button.
- Each option shows its date, completeness (in words and with an icon, never colour alone), total size and identity
  confidence and basis.
- If either snapshot is Incomplete, a banner explains: "Some locations could not be read in one of these scans. Items
  there are shown as **Unknown**, never as Missing."
- Below Strong confidence, or with basis Location or UserAsserted, the ID-09 banner is shown.
- A clock anomaly (§12.1) is shown with both dates.

### 14.5 Presentation rules (UI-04 to UI-09, UI-11)

- **UI-04:** each class has a word, an icon and a colour; the Missing help says "not found when its folder was
  listed during the newer scan" (a scan is not instantaneous). Unknown always shows its reason in plain words:
  - AncestorUnreadable: "Couldn't read this folder in the newer (older) scan";
  - ListingIncomplete: "The folder's listing stopped part-way";
  - BehindLink: "Behind a link (not followed)";
  - NameVariantPresent: "Another item here has the same name apart from letter case or accents, so StorageInventory
    can't tell whether this is the same item";
  - NameMatchingUnverified: "This drive's rules for matching names (letter case, accents) aren't verified, so
    StorageInventory can't prove this item is gone" (or "is new");
  - ChangeIndeterminate: "Same size, time moved by whole hours on a FAT/exFAT drive: possibly a clock change".
- **UI-05:** incomplete snapshots are always labelled Incomplete, and their totals say "at least".
- **UI-06:** offline sources show when and where they were last seen; there is never a spinner for "checking" network
  drives.
- **UI-07:** the Library's own folder is labelled "StorageInventory Library (app data)" wherever it appears inside a
  scanned source, with help text about the one-snapshot lag and about listing sizes of open files (§6.5 rule 4).
- **UI-08:** every Library state (§6.8) and every `CaptureFailureKind` (§9.4) has a fixed message with a "What you can
  do" line; a test asserts that the table covers every value (TEST-U3).
- **UI-09 (accessibility):** everything v1 has is carried forward (word + icon + colour; access keys, visible labels and
  automation names; polite live regions for progress; `ThemeMode="System"` light and dark; PerMonitorV2 DPI). New for
  v1.1: every chart has a data table; diff tabs and lists are virtualised and keyboard navigable.
- **UI-11:** a Library state banner appears on every page whenever the state is not Available, and actions that need
  the Library are disabled with the state's reason.

### 14.6 Behaviour-preserving decomposition first (C2)

`MainWindow.xaml` (388 lines) is split into `SetupView`, `ScanProgressView` and `ResultsView` user controls, bound to
the **existing** view models and hosted inside the new shell's Scan page; the scan moves into `ScanSession` (UI-12).
- **Acceptance:** every v1 UI test passes unchanged; rendered screenshots of the three stages (light and dark, on the
  synthetic fixture) differ from v1 **only in the shell chrome** (the navigation list and frame), confirmed by an
  image diff masked to the content region; the smoke test passes.
- **No new pages in C2.**

---

## 15. Performance and capacity plan

Every figure below is labelled **measured** (with where and how) or **projected** (with its basis). No projected figure
may appear in user documentation until the named gate has measured it (REV-O08).

### 15.1 Measured

**(a) The `c0c031f` probe (Windows).** Machine: 12th Gen Intel Core i7-1270P, 32 GB, internal SSD, Windows 11 26200 (the
machine of `docs/benchmarks/native.md`). A disposable harness using **Windows' `winsqlite3.dll` 3.51.1 as a proxy
engine**; synthetic trees at about 4.3 files per folder (the reference drive's ratio: 2,009,918 files in 467,481
folders); the §9 schema in a TEXT-name variant; WAL, `synchronous=NORMAL` for 50,000-row batches and `FULL` for
publish; no foreign keys on observation tables; the snapshot written **alone**, not during a scan; **insertion order
not recorded**; one and two snapshots only.

| Files per snapshot | Write snapshot 1 | Write snapshot 2 | DB size after 1 / after 2 | Bytes per file observation (marginal) | Ordered-merge compare (2 snapshots) | Search: SQL LIKE on all rows / names first | Top-1000 by size | `sum(size)` |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 250,000 | 1.38 s (181k rows/s) | 0.98 s | 17.4 / 32.1 MB | 61 B | 0.19 s | 0.19 s / — | 0.04 s | 0.04 s |
| 1,000,000 | 5.42 s (185k rows/s) | 4.18 s | 72.9 / 133.5 MB | 63 B | 1.09 s | 1.42 s / — | 0.31 s | 0.18 s |
| 2,000,000 | 10.33 s (194k rows/s) | 12.82 s | 147.0 / 268.9 MB | 64 B | 3.33 s | 4.92 s / **0.56 s** | 0.43 s | 0.65 s |

Maintenance at 2M files (same probe): `quick_check` 2.51 s; `integrity_check` 4.43 s; deleting one snapshot in one
transaction 3.11 s; `VACUUM` afterwards 4.82 s (268.9 → 134.2 MB). The merge reproduced the injected ground truth
exactly at every size (at 2M: 20,157 changed, 10,063 removed, 10,000 added).

**(b) The repair probe (Linux, relative only) [E-3].** Python `sqlite3` (SQLite 3.45.1), a `file_obs`-shaped table
only, 1M rows per snapshot, each import in **one** transaction:

| Mode | Order | Import 1 / import 2 | Peak side file during import | Delete one snapshot (peak side file) | Re-import into freed pages (peak side file) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| rollback `TRUNCATE` | key | 3.48 s / 3.22 s | journal 0.0 MB | 0.41 s (journal 31.5 MB) | 2.61 s (journal 0.1 MB) |
| rollback `TRUNCATE` | shuffled | 8.02 s / 8.24 s | journal 0.0 MB | 0.74 s (journal 30.7 MB) | 7.28 s (journal 0.1 MB) |
| WAL | key | 3.25 s / 3.28 s | WAL 31.6 / 32.7 MB | 0.38 s (WAL 31.6 MB) | 3.18 s (WAL 32.7 MB) |
| WAL | shuffled | 9.39 s / 8.67 s | WAL 30.8 / 31.6 MB | 0.39 s (WAL 30.8 MB) | 9.06 s (WAL 31.7 MB) |

Readings: key order is 2.3 to 2.9 times faster than shuffled order in both modes; rollback mode needs almost no journal
for an appending import; the two modes are equally fast. Absolute numbers do not transfer to Windows.

**Not measured anywhere yet:** the shipped engine (3.53.3) on Windows; UTF-16 `BLOB` names; the `seq` and
`discovery_index` columns; traversal-order capture through the spool; capture concurrent with a real scan; more than two
snapshots; comparison with folder alignment and case-fold checks; memory.

### 15.2 Capacity projection (full snapshots, D-07)

**Projected** linearly from the measured marginal cost (61 to 64 B per file observation) plus about 3 to 4 B for `seq`
(projected); not measured beyond two snapshots.

| Source size | First snapshot | Each further snapshot | 10 snapshots | 50 snapshots | 100 snapshots |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 250k files | ~17 MB | ~15 MB | ~0.15 GB | ~0.8 GB | ~1.5 GB |
| 2M files (the reference system drive) | ~150 MB | ~135 MB | ~1.4 GB | ~6.8 GB | ~13.5 GB |

- **Index overhead.** `file_obs` and `folder_obs` are clustered `WITHOUT ROWID` tables with no secondary index, so
  index overhead grows with distinct names and paths (the `name` table stores each distinct name twice: row and
  `UNIQUE` index), not with the number of snapshots.
- **Fill and fragmentation.** `snapshot_id` leads each key, so a new snapshot appends at the right edge; key-ordered
  import (IMP-03) keeps its pages dense. After deletions, later imports reuse freed pages [E-3], scattering a
  snapshot's pages: harmless on SSD, measured on HDD by CAP-03's churn test.
- **Deletion cost.** Measured 3.11 s at 2M on the proxy; in rollback mode deletion journals about the deleted
  snapshot's size [E-3], so it needs that much free space transiently. The file does not shrink until Compact (1.1.x).
- **Transient disk needs.** Import: almost no journal, plus the snapshot's own growth. Deletion: a journal of about one
  snapshot. Capture: the spool in the report folder, about 80 B per file (projected).
- **Backup implications.** StorageInventory does not back up the Library in 1.1.0 (SREQ-05 is 1.1.x). History is lost
  if the drive holding `%LOCALAPPDATA%` fails. A user copy of the closed Library's `library.sqlite3` is complete (§6.3)
  and is the size of the Library.
- **Scale claims.** Every figure in this table is a projection. Before release, C10 measures a **separate series**
  of Libraries with 1, 10 and 50 snapshots of a **1M-file** source (TEST-P3); CAP-05 compares that series with this
  projection scaled to 1M files. The table's own rows (250k and 2M files) and its 100-snapshot column stay projections
  and are never documented as characteristics (G0-O04).

### 15.3 Budgets (proposed; each confirmed or revised with measured justification at its gate)

| ID | Budget | Basis | Confirmed in |
|---|---|---|---|
| PERF-01 | Import throughput **≥ 100,000 file rows/s** in key order | measured 181k–194k rows/s (proxy, WAL, written alone); relative: rollback = WAL [E-3] | C4 (engine), C5 (end to end) |
| PERF-02a | Scan with Save on, warm-cache 250k-file tree, sorted mode: time until the reports are ready **≤ 1.10×** v1 (v1: 6.36 s, `docs/benchmarks/native.md`) | projected: the only addition during the scan is sequential spool appends | C5 |
| PERF-02b | Same tree: time until "Saved to History" **≤ 1.5×** v1 | projected: v1 time plus about 1.5 s of import at the measured rate | C5 |
| PERF-03 | Library growth **≤ 90 B per file observation**, folder rows, interning and `seq` included | measured 61–64 B without `seq`; projected +3–4 B | C5 |
| PERF-04 | Search over the latest snapshot of a 2M-file source: **≤ 2 s** to the first page | measured 0.56 s (proxy) | C9 |
| PERF-05 | Compare two 2M-file snapshots: **≤ 10 s** to the summary, including folder alignment, case-fold checks and input verification | measured merge 3.3 s (proxy) | C7 (synthetic), C10 (Library) |
| PERF-06 | The comparison's in-memory list of non-Unchanged items holds at most **1,000,000 entries and ≤ 200 MB**; beyond that, summary and top-N only (§12.8) | design limit | C7 |
| PERF-07 | History timeline of a source with 50 snapshots: **≤ 0.5 s** | projected | C10 (50 × 1M Library) |
| PERF-08 | Opening the Library at start-up with 50 snapshots of 1M files: **≤ 1 s**, with no integrity check at open | projected | C10 |
| PERF-10 | Deleting a 2M-file snapshot: **≤ 10 s** | measured 3.1 s (proxy, WAL) | C6, C10 |
| PERF-11a | Peak working set during a scan with capture: **≤ v1 peak + 64 MB** at 2M files (spool buffer and run index) | projected | C5 |
| PERF-11b | Peak working set during the import: **≤ v1 peak + 300 MB** at 2M files (IMP-08) | projected | C5 |
| PERF-12 | Comparison memory: folder alignment **≤ 300 MB** at 2 × 470k folders, plus **≤ 120 B per file** of the largest single folder pair | projected | C7, C10 |
| PERF-13 | Spool size **≤ 120 B per file** observation | projected about 80 B | C5 |
| PERF-14 | Saving phase (P5 to P7) at 2M files, including the verification pass V (SPOOL-23): **≤ 60 s** | projected 10–15 s (IMP-09) plus two sequential reads of the spool (V; the error pass of IMP-03) | C5 (Q-17 in C4 for the engine part) |
